Derived Log Albanese sheaves Thanks: F.B. is supported by the PRIN “Geometric, Algebraic and Analytic Methods in Arithmetic”. Thanks: A.M. is supported by the Swiss National Science Foundation (SNSF), project 200020_178729
Federico Binda, Alberto Merici and Shuji Saito

Abstract
. We define higher pro-Albanese functors for every effective log motive over a field k k of characteristic zero, and we compute them for every smooth log smooth scheme X = ( X ¯ , ∂ X ) X=(\underline{X},\partial X) . The result involves an inverse system of the coherent cohomology of the underlying scheme as well as a pro-group scheme Alb log ​ ( X ) \mathrm{Alb}^{\log}(X) that extends Serre’s semi-abelian Albanese variety of X ¯ − | ∂ X | \underline{X}-|\partial X| . This generalizes the higher Albanese sheaves of Ayoub, Barbieri-Viale and Kahn and is related to an old question of Grothendieck.



1.
Introduction

Let
k
be a perfect field and let
X
be a smooth, quasi-projective and geometrically connected
k
-scheme. A very classical tool in the study of the geometry of
X
is given by the
Albanese variety

\operatorname{Alb}_{X}
of
X
, the universal Abelian variety receiving a map from
X
(up to the choice of a base point). When
X
is a smooth curve, the Albanese variety coincides with the Jacobian variety
{\rm Jac}(X)
of
X
, and essentially every invariant of
X
can be recovered from it.
In higher dimension, the Albanese variety is still an important tool for gathering information about the Chow group
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(X)
of zero cycles of
X
. Extending the Albanese map by linearity, there is in fact a well-defined morphism (now independent on the choice of a base point)

(1.0.1)

{a}_{X}\colon{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(X)^{0}\to\operatorname{Alb}_{X}(k).

Much is known, at least conjecturally, about this map. If
X
is proper over an algebraically closed field, a famous theorem of Rojtman
[
Roj80
]
asserts that
{a}_{X}
is an isomorphism on torsion subgroups (at least modulo
p
-torsion in characteristic
p>0
, which was later fixed by Milne
[
Mil82
]
). If
k
is finite, the kernel of
a_{X}
can be explicitly determined by geometric class field theory
[
KS83
]
. If
k
is the algebraic closure of a finite field, then a theorem of Kato and Saito (see again
[
KS83
]
) asserts that
{a}_{X}
is in fact an isomorphism, a statement that is conjectured to be true even when
k=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
as consequence of the Bloch-Beilinson conjectures. This is far from being true over the complex numbers, as shown by Mumford.

When
X
is no longer proper, both sides of (
1.0.1
) need to be modified. It is already clear from the case of curves
[
Ser75
]
that one can consider a more general class of commutative algebraic groups as target of a map from
X
, including Abelian varieties, tori, and their extensions, i.e. semi-Abelian varieties. Serre
[
Ser60
]
(see also
[
FW84
]
) showed that the problem of finding a universal map to a semi-Abelian variety has always a solution. The corresponding universal object is now known as Serre’s Albanese variety: it agrees with
the usual Albanese variety if
X
is proper.

Using Serre’s semi-Abelian Albanese variety it is possible to extend the Albanese morphism to every smooth quasi-projective variety
1
1

1

at least after inverting the exponential characteristic of the ground field, in an appropriate sense.
. As observed by Spieß and Szamuely
[
SS03
]
, every semi-Abelian variety, seen as étale sheaf on the big site
\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, has a natural structure of étale sheaf with transfers, i.e. it enjoys an extra functoriality with respect to the category of finite correspondences
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)
introduced by Suslin and Voevodsky. Since every map from an affine space to a torus or to an Abelian variety is constant, such sheaves are moreover
{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
-homotopy invariant.

These two facts are essentially enough to show that the assignment
X\mapsto\mathbf{Alb}_{X}
(here
\mathbf{Alb}_{X}
is the non-connected algebraic group whose neutral component is exactly Serre’s semi-Abelian Albanese) can be promoted by left Kan extension to a motivic “realization” functor

(1.0.2)

L\operatorname{Alb}\colon\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

defined on the
\infty
-category of Voevodsky’s effective motives
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, i.e. the full subcategory of the derived
\infty
-category of étale sheaves with transfers
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
whose objects are
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local complexes, taking values in the derived
\infty
-category of the Abelian category
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
of
1
-motivic sheaves with rational coefficients: this is the full subcategory of étale sheaves with transfers generated under colimits by lattices (i.e. étale sheaves
L
such that
L(\overline{k})\cong\mathbb{Z}^{r}
) and semi-abelian varieties (see
[
ABV09
, Prop. 1.3.8]
). It is naturally a full subcategory of the abelian category of homotopy invariant sheaves with transfers.
This result can in fact be refined to integral coefficients by considering a more exotic (and probably not fully faithful) functor

R\iota\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}})\to\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}),

and by constructing
L\operatorname{Alb}
as its left adjoint (this functor is in fact much more mysterious, and we will not take it in consideration). This result, due to Ayoub and Barbieri-Viale
[
ABV09
, Thm. 2.4.1]
(extending Barbieri-Viale and Kahn
[
BVK16
]
to non necessarily geometric motives) has several consequences. First, it provides a construction of an Albanese map for arbitrary motives (in particular, for every separated
k
-scheme of finite type, not necessarily smooth or proper), giving for example vast generalizations of the theorem of Rojtman
[
BVK16
, 13]
. Second, the Albanese functor is now a
derived
functor: it has higher homotopy groups
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}(M)=\pi_{i}(L\operatorname{Alb}(M))
for every
M\in\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, encoding information such as the Néron-Severi group of a variety (see
[
BVK16
, Thm. 9.2.3]
).
Moreover, the functor
L\operatorname{Alb}
in (
1.0.2
) can be identified with the left adjoint of the derived functor
i_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{DM}^{eff}}}
of the natural embedding
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. One can show that
i_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{DM}^{eff}}}
is fully faithful, and its essential image coincides with the stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
generated by the motives of curves. If we restrict ourselves to compact objects,
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))^{\omega}
coincides with the (bounded) derived category of the Abelian category of Deligne
1
-motives introduced in
[
Del74
]
. In fact, the properties of (
1.0.2
) are essential in the “motivic” proof of Deligne’s conjectures on 1-motives, see
[
BVK16
, Part 4]
and
[
Vol13
]
.

Our goal in this paper is to extend the picture sketched above in order to include a more general kind of algebraic groups in the definition of the Albanese variety. Thanks to Chevalley’s structure theorem, any connected commutative algebraic group over a perfect field can be written as an extension of an Abelian variety by an affine smooth group scheme, which splits as a product of a torus by a unipotent commutative group. As observed by Serre, however, the problem of finding a universal map from a smooth variety
X
to an arbitrary commutative algebraic group does
not
have a solution in general (namely, when
X
is not proper), whence the classical restriction to semi-Abelian varieties. A solution does, however, exist, if a bound on the dimension of the tangent spaces of the groups is imposed.
Let us assume that
k
has characteristic zero (and keep this assumption throughout the rest of the Introduction, see Proposition
3.20
). Faltings and Wüstholz
[
FW84
]
realized that when
X
admits a smooth compactification
\overline{X}
with normal crossing boundary
D
, it is possible to use any finite dimensional subspace of the vector space
H^{0}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})
to give such a bound. A natural choice is to use for
n\geq 1
the subspaces
H^{0}(\overline{X},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}}(nD))
of regular
1
-forms on
X
having poles of order at most
n
along
D
. The resulting universal object
\operatorname{Alb}_{(\overline{X},nD)}
depends on the pair
\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}:=(\overline{X},nD)
in a functorial way. This gives a generalized Albanese morphism

a_{\mathfrak{X}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\colon\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},

which is a surjective morphism of étale sheaves with transfers with rational coefficients (here
\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X)
denotes the étale sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces represented by
X
). The generalized Albanese
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}
is an extension of Serre’s semi-Abelian Albanese of
X
(independent on the choice of the compactification
\overline{X}
) by a unipotent group. If
X
is a curve,
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}={\operatorname{Jac}}(\overline{X},nD)
is exactly the generalised Jacobian variety of Rosenlicht and Serre
[
Ser75
]
, and in higher dimension it is the generalised Albanese with modulus considered in
[
BS19
]
,
[
BK18
]
(see also
[
Rus08
]
,
[
Rus13
]
).

By varying
n
, we get a pro-object in the category of commutative algebraic groups up to isogeny
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}
, which satisfies an obvious universal property, see Prop.
3.22
.

In fact, we can give a finer result. Let
\mathbf{RSC}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
be the full (abelian) subcategory of the category of étale sheaves with transfers
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Q})
generated under colimits by commutative connected
k
-group schemes of finite type and lattices. Note that we clearly have
\mathbf{HI}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subset\mathbf{RSC}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. Write
\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
for the category of normal compactifications
\overline{X}
of
X
such that the complement
\overline{X}-X
is the support of an effective Cartier divisor.

Theorem 1.1
.

(see Thm.
4.26
) Assume that the characteristic of
k
is zero.
The embedding
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
has a pro-left adjoint:

(1.1.1)

\operatorname{Alb}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}),

induced by colimit from

\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\mapsto``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}

for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
smooth.

It is natural to ask for a derived version of the above Theorem, in the spirit of the result of Ayoub, Barbieri-Viale and Kahn. However, since unipotent group schemes are
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-contractible, (
1.1.1
) cannot be extended to
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
in a non-trivial way, i.e. without simply collapsing to the subcategory
\mathbf{HI}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, recovering (
1.0.2
).

Our solution to this difficulty is to extend the construction to a framework in which
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-contractibility is no longer a problem. This is achieved by passing from the world of algebraic geometry to the world of
logarithmic algebraic geometry
, in the sense of Fontaine, Illusie, Kato and others. Over a field
k
(seen as log scheme with trivial log structure), we can, roughly speaking, replace schemes with log pairs
X:=(\underline{X},\partial X)
, where
\underline{X}
is the
underlying

k
-scheme and
\partial X
is a log structure supported on a Cartier divisor (the so-called compactifying log structure associated to the open embedding of schemes
\underline{X}-|\partial X|\hookrightarrow\underline{X}
).

For
X
a smooth log-smooth scheme (i.e. a log scheme such that the underlying scheme is smooth and the log structure is supported on a normal crossing divisor, see
2.1.1
), we write
X^{\circ}:={\underline{X}}-|\partial X|
and
\mathbf{Comp}(X)
for the category of normal compactifications
\overline{X}
of
\underline{X}
such that
\overline{X}-X^{\circ}=|\partial X|+D
, for
D
an effective Cartier divisor. We can then consider the pro-algebraic group

\mathbf{Alb}_{X}\coloneqq``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"_{n}\mathbf{Alb}_{(\overline{X},|\partial X|_{\rm red}+nD)}

as an invariant of the log scheme
X
. Note that we recover the previous pro-Albanese in the case where
X=(\underline{X},\mathrm{triv})
, i.e. the scheme
\underline{X}
seen as log scheme with trivial log structure.

In order to exploit this formalism, we need another observation. Any commutative group scheme
G
(not just semi-Abelian varieties) gives rise to an étale sheaf with transfers (still denoted
G
) on
\mathbf{Sm}(k)
. As such, it belongs to the subcategory
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Z})
of
reciprocity sheaves
. Its objects satisfy the property that each section
a\in F(X)
for any
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
“has bounded ramification”, i.e. that the corresponding map
a\colon\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to F
factors through a quotient
h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})
associated to a pair
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},D)
where
\overline{X}
is a proper compactification of
X
and
D
is an effective Cartier divisor such that
X=\overline{X}-|D|
(we refer to such a pair as a Cartier compactification of
X
). Thanks to
[
Sai21
]
, every reciprocity sheaf
F
is
logarithmic
, i.e. it can be extended in a unique way to a functor
\mathcal{L}og(F)
defined on the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
of smooth log smooth log schemes over
k
(see also
[
BM21
]
for an alternative construction).
In fact, we have that (with rational coefficients)

\mathcal{L}og(F)\in\mathbf{logCI}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Q})

where
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is the category of
dividing étale sheaves with log transfers
introduced in
[
BPØ22b
, Section 3]
(see also
[
BPØ22a
]
), i.e. sheaves for a certain Grothendieck topology on the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
of log smooth log schemes over
k
, equipped with an extra transfer structure with respect to an extension of Voevodsky’s category of finite correspondences. The topology is generated by étale covers of the underlying schemes together with admissible blow-ups with center contained in the locus where the log structure is non-trivial. The category
\mathbf{logCI}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
is the Grothendieck abelian category
[
BM21
, Thm. 5.7]
of strictly
{\overline{\square}}:=(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)
-invariant sheaves (here
(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)
denotes the log scheme
\mathbf{P}^{1}
with compactifying log structure given by the open embedding
\mathbf{A}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{1}
).
Again by
[
BM21
, Thm. 5.7]
, it is the heart of a t-structure, called the homotopy t-structure, on the
\infty
-category of effective log motives
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})

[
BPØ22b
]
, i.e. the full subcategory of the derived
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
consisting of
{\overline{\square}}
-local complexes.
By Saito’s theorem, we have actually a fully faithful embedding (see (
2.10.1
))

(1.1.2)

\omega_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{logCI}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Q}),

and passing to the derived
\infty
-categories, a functor

\omega_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\xrightarrow{L_{{\overline{\square}}}}\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}),

where
L_{{\overline{\square}}}
is the localization functor.

If we put these facts together, we see that for each
(\underline{X},\partial X)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, each smooth compactification
(\overline{X},|\partial X|+D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
and
n\geq 1
, we can construct a strictly
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant sheaf
\omega_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{(\overline{X},|\partial X|_{\rm red}+nD)})
defined on the category of log smooth log schemes over
k
. This extends to the motivic category in the following way.

Theorem 1.2

(Theorems
6.1
and
7.3
, Proposition
7.4
)
.

Assume that the characteristic of
k
is zero.
The functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
has a pro-left adjoint, the log motivic Albanese functor:

L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})),

where
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}
means the pro-
\infty
-category, which fits in a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\operatorname{Alb}_{\leq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}}

where
L\operatorname{Alb}_{\leq 1}
is the (restriction of the) functor
L\operatorname{Alb}
of Ayoub, Barbieri-Viale and Kahn (
1.0.2
),
\omega^{*}
is the natural comparison functor

\omega^{*}\colon\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}),

which is fully faithful by
[
BPØ22b
, Thm.8.2.16]
.
Moreover, the functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
is fully faithful and its essential image is the full stable
\infty
-subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
generated by
\omega^{*}\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
and
\mathbf{G}_{a}[n]
.

The proof of the above theorem is fairly technical, and requires new ingredients compared to the argument given in
[
ABV09
]
(among which some very explicit computations). We would like to stress that the formalism of stable
\infty
-categories is essential to generalize the usual construction of derived functors (via resolutions) to pro-adjoint functors between derived categories, as we explain in Appendix
A
.

Theorem
1.2
in particular asserts that for all
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
and
G
a commutative algebraic group (with rational coefficients), we have an equivalence

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X,G)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X,\mathrm{triv}),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(G))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X,\mathrm{triv}),G).

In particular, the pro-object
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X,\mathrm{triv})
represents the étale cohomology of
G
. A similar object was considered in a letter from Grothendieck to Serre
[
GSC01
, August 9, 1960]
, where for every smooth variety
X
, a complex of pro-agebraic groups
J_{*}
was constructed by the use of local cohomology, such that
\operatorname{Hom}(J_{*},G)
computes the Zariski cohomology of
G
. The reference to the existence of this object was suggested to us by B. Toën, we thank him for this.

For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, we determine the homotopy groups
\pi_{i}L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)
completely:

Theorem 1.3

(Theorem
6.13
)
.

Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
geometrically connected and
(\overline{X},D)
a Cartier compactification of
X
. Then we have that

\pi_{i}L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\cong\begin{cases}``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"(H^{i}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(nD))^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a})&\textrm{for }2\leq i\leq\dim(X)\\
\begin{subarray}{c}``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"\Bigl((H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(nD))/H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}))^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}\Bigr)\\
\oplus{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\underline{X}-|\partial X|)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\end{subarray}&\textrm{for }i=1\\
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)&\textrm{for }i=0\\
0&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

where
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\underline{X}-|\partial X|)
is the dual torus to the Néron-Severi group of
\underline{X}-|\partial X|
, and for
V
a
k
-vector space,
V^{\vee}
denotes its linear dual.

As an application, we can identify the compact objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}_{\leq 1}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
: our result generalizes
[
BVK16
]
on Deligne
1
-motives to the case of étale Laumon
1
-motives:

Theorem 1.4

(Theorem
8.16
)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}
be the category of étale Laumon
1
-motives (see
8.1
and
8.3
). Then the functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
preserves compact objects and it induces an equivalence

\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{log}{\mathcal{DM}}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1,\rm gm}(k,\mathbb{Q})

where the right-hand side is the
\infty
-subcategory of compact objects of
\mathbf{log}{\mathcal{DM}}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})

Finally, we remark that it is essential to use the étale topology with rational coefficients from the beginning in Theorem
1.1
for the following reason: the map
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is surjective in the étale topology (and not in the Nisnevich topology), which forces us to work in the category of étale reciprocity sheaves (a similar issue occurs in Ayoub and Barbieri-Viale, where they are forced to work in
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
). On the other hand, it is not known in general that the category of étale reciprocity sheaves is closed under quotients, and the reason is the following: if
X_{x}^{h}
is an Henselian local scheme, then its generic point is again Henselian local. On the other hand, if
X_{x}^{sh}
is a Henselian, its generic point is not going to be be strictly Henselian anymore. This forces us to use rational coefficients to exploit Voevodsky’s theorem and conclude that
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is an abelian category.

1.1.
Outline

We now give a brief outline of the contents of the various sections of this paper.

In Section
2
, we give a quick reminder of the theory of reciprocity sheaves and modulus sheaves with transfers as developed in
[
KMSY21a
]
,
[
KMSY21b
]
,
[
KSY21
]
and
[
Sai20
]
. We also give a quick recollection of the material in
[
BPØ22b
]
and
[
BM21
]
on logarithmic motives and we prove some basic result with rational coefficients.
In Section
3
, we construct the Albanese map with modulus as a universal object in the category of reciprocity sheaves and compare it with the usual Albanese map and the Albanese group scheme of
[
BK18
]
.
In Section
4
we introduce the categories of
n
-reciprocity sheaves by a suitable modification of the techniques of
[
ABV09
]
. We prove that the category of
0
-reciprocity sheaves agrees with the category of
0
-motivic sheaves of
[
ABV09
]
. We show the existence of a pro-left adjoint
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
of the fully faithful embedding of
1
-motivic sheaves into the category of dividing étale sheaves with log transfers, or “logarithmic sheaves” for short (again with rational coefficients).

Section
6
is the most technical one: we prove that the category of logarithmic sheaves admits enough
BC
-admissible objects (in the sense of the Appendix
A
) and deduce the existence of a pro-left derived functor
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
. The techniques in particular are fairly different from the corresponding one in
[
ABV09
]
, although the general structure of the proof is similar. Next, we prove that the functor
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
factors through
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}
and that on
\mathcal{DM}_{\leq 1}^{\rm eff}
it agrees with the motivic Albanese map of
[
ABV09
]
(note that this result is optimal, see Remark
6.21
). After that, we compute
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\mathbf{G}_{a})
thanks to an explicit resolution (the Breen-Deligne resolution of the algebraic group
\mathbf{G}_{a}
), deducing the full faithfulness of the inclusion
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}})(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

In Section
6.3
, we perform several computations, and we identify precisely
L\operatorname{Alb}(X)
for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, proving Theorem
1.3
. We also pose some questions about the behaviour of the higher pro-Albanese sheaves in some special geometric situations.
In Section
8
we consider the category of étale Laumon
1
-motives, and prove that they are motivic in the sense that their bounded derived category agrees with the category of compact objects in the category of logarithmic 1-motives, as explained in Theorem
1.4
.

Finally, in the Appendix
A
we introduce the notion of
BC
-admissible objects in a stable
\infty
-category and generalise the notion of a derived functor to pro-adjuntions between derived stable
\infty
-categories which are not in general induced by Quillen adjunctions.

2.
Reciprocity sheaves and logarithmic motives with rational coefficients

We work over a fixed ground field
k
, which is assumed to be perfect. Let
\Lambda
be a (commutative) ring of coefficients.
In this section, we recall the main results on reciprocity sheaves and logarithmic motives and we state some general results on the categories with rational coefficients.

Let
\mathbf{Sm}(k)
be the category of separated smooth schemes of finite type over
k
, and let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)
be the additive category of finite correspondences. It has the same objects as
\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, and for
X,Y\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, the hom group
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)
is the free abelian group on the set of integral closed subschemes of
X\times Y
which are finite and surjective over a connected component of
X
(see
[
MVW06
, Def. 1.1]
). We denote by
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
the category of additive presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)
, whose objects are called
presheaves with transfers
. For
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, we let
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)=\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(-,X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda
be the representable object. For
\tau
the Nisnevich or the étale topology, we let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
be the category of
\tau
-sheaves with transfers and we let

a_{\tau}^{V}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)

be Voevodsky’s
\tau
-sheafification functor: it is induced by the classical sheafification functor defined on the category of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules without transfers.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
be the category of
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant presheaves, i.e. objects
F
such that the projection
X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\to X
induces an isomorphism
F(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}F(X)
for every
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
. Set
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\tau}=\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
.

We recall the following result:

Proposition 2.1

(
[
MVW06
]
, Cor. 14.22, Prop. 14.23)
.

Let
\Lambda
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra. Then for every
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
we have
a_{\rm Nis}F=a_{\rm\acute{e}t}F
. Moreover, for all smooth
X
and
n>0
we have

H^{n}_{\rm Nis}(X,F)=H^{n}_{\rm\acute{e}t}(X,F).

2.1.
The
\infty
-category of logarithmic motives

We recall the construction of the
\infty
-category of logarithmic motives of
[
BPØ22b
]
and some properties. The standard reference for log schemes is
[
Ogu18
]
.
We denote by
{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
the category of fine and saturated (fs for short) log smooth log schemes over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
, considered as a log scheme with trivial log structure.

2.1.1.
Log geometry

For
X\in{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
, we write
{\underline{X}}\in\mathbf{Sch}(k)
for the underlying
k
-scheme. We also write
\partial X
for the (closed) subset of
{\underline{X}}
where the log structure of
X
is not trivial. Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
having for objects
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
such that
{\underline{X}}
is smooth over
k
. By e.g.
[
BPØ22b
, A.5.10]
, if
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, then
\partial X
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
{\underline{X}}
and the log scheme
X
is isomorphic to
({\underline{X}},\partial{X})
, i.e. to the compactifying log structure associated to the open embedding
({\underline{X}}\setminus\partial X)\to{\underline{X}}
. If
X,Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, we will write
X\times Y
for the fiber product of
X
and
Y
over
k
computed in the category of fine and saturated log schemes: it exists by
[
Ogu18
, Cor. III.2.1.6]
and it is again an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
using
[
Ogu18
, Cor. IV.3.1.11]
. Since
k
has trivial log structure, the underlying scheme
{\underline{X\times Y}}
agrees with
{\underline{X}}\times_{k}{\underline{Y}}
and the support of
\partial(X\times Y)
is
|\partial X|\times{\underline{Y}}\cup{\underline{X}}\times|\partial Y|
, in particular
X\times Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
. See
[
Ogu18
, §III.2.1]
for more details.

A morphism
f\colon X\to Y
of fs log schemes is called
strict
if the log structure on
X
is the pullback log structure from
Y
. Geometrically, if both
X
and
Y
are objects in
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, this amounts to require that there is an equality
\partial X=f^{*}(\partial Y)
as reduced normal crossing divisors on
X
. For
\tau
a Grothendieck topology on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}(k)
, the
\emph{strict}
topology
s\tau
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
is the Grothendieck topology generated by covers
\{e_{i}\colon X_{i}\to X\}
such that
\underline{e_{i}}\colon\underline{X_{i}}\to\underline{X}
is a
\tau
-cover and each
e_{i}
is strict. Recall from
[
BPØ22b
, 3.1.4]
that a cartesian square of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

is a
dividing distinguished square
(or
elementary dividing square
) if
Y^{\prime}=X^{\prime}=\emptyset
and
f
is a log modifications, in the sense of F. Kato
[
Kat21
]
(see
[
BPØ22b
, A.11.9]
for more details on log modifications). The collection of dividing distinguished squares forms a cd structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, called the
dividing cd structure
. For
\tau
a Grothendieck topology on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}(k)
, the
\emph{dividing}
topology
d\tau
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
is the topology on
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
generated by the strict topology
s\tau
and the dividing cd structure.

From now until the end of the section, we will consider
\tau\in\{{\operatorname{Nis}},{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}\}
.

2.1.2.
Correspondences and transfers

Following
[
BPØ22b
]
, we denote by
\operatorname{\mathbf{lCor}}(k)
the category of finite log correspondences over
k
. It is a variant of the Suslin–Voevodsky category of finite correspondences
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)
. It has the same objects as
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
2
2

2

Notice that this notation conflicts with the notation of
[
BPØ22b
]
where the objects were the same as
\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
, although the categories of sheaves are the same in light of
[
BPØ22b
, Lemma 4.7.2]
, and morphisms are given by the free abelian subgroup

\operatorname{\mathbf{lCor}}(X,Y)\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X-\partial X,Y-\partial Y)

generated by elementary correspondences
V^{o}\subset(X-\partial X)\times(Y-\partial Y)
such that the closure
V\subset{\underline{X}}\times{\underline{Y}}
is finite and surjective over (a component of)
{\underline{X}}
and such that there exists a morphism of log schemes
V^{N}\to Y
, where
V^{N}
is the fs log scheme whose underlying scheme is the normalization of
V
and whose log structure is given by the inverse image log structure along the composition
\underline{V^{N}}\to{\underline{X}}\times{\underline{Y}}\to{\underline{X}}
. See
[
BPØ22b
, 2.1]
for more details, and for the proof that this definition gives indeed a category.

Additive presheaves (of
\Lambda
-modules) on the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{lCor}}(k)
will be called
presheaves (of
\Lambda
-modules) with log transfers
. Write
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}^{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
for the resulting category. As usual, for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{lCor}}(k)
we denote by
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
the representable presheaf
\operatorname{\mathbf{lCor}}(-,X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda
.
As in
[
BM21
]
, we let
\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}}(k)
be the category of fs log smooth
k
-schemes
X
which are essentially smooth over
k
, i.e.
X
is a limit
\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i\in I}X_{i}
over a filtered set
I
,
where
X_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and all transition maps are strict étale (i.e. they are strict maps of log schemes such that the underlying maps
f_{ij}\colon{\underline{X}}_{i}\to{\underline{X}}_{j}
are affine and étale). For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and
x\in\underline{X}
, we put

(2.1.1)

X_{x}^{h}=(\underline{X}_{x}^{h},\partial X_{x}^{h})\in\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}}(k)

where
(\underline{X})_{x}^{h}
denotes the henselization of
X
at
x
and
(\partial X)_{x}^{h}
denotes the pullback of
\partial X
along the henselization map. For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}^{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
X\in\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}}(k)
such that
X=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i\in I}X_{i}
for
X_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
we put as usual

F(X):=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i\in I}F(X_{i})
.

We denote by
\mathbf{Shv}_{d\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}^{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
the subcategory of
d\tau
-sheaves. By
[
BPØ22b
, Prop. 4.5.4]
and
[
BPØ22b
, Thm. 4.5.7]
, the inclusion
\mathbf{Shv}_{d\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}^{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
admits an exact left adjoint
a_{d\tau}
(see
[
BPØ22b
, Prop. 4.2.10]
), and the category
\mathbf{Shv}_{d\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)
is a Grothendieck abelian category (
[
BPØ22b
, Prop. 4.2.12]
). For
\tau\in\{{\operatorname{Nis}},{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}\}
,
[
BPØ22b
,
Theorem 5.1.8]
implies that for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}^{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
,

(2.1.2)

H^{i}_{d\tau}(X,a_{d\tau}F)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{Y\in X_{\rm div}^{\mathbf{Sm}}}H^{i}_{s\tau}(Y,a_{s\tau}F).

where
X_{\rm div}^{\mathbf{Sm}}
is the filtered category of log modifications
Y\to X
such that
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
. The following statement can be shown by imitating the proof of
[
MVW06
, Prop. 14.23]
using (
2.1.2
).

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
\Lambda
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra and let
F
be an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

H^{n}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,a_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}F)=H^{n}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(X,a_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F),

for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and
n\geq 0
.

Finally the monoidal structure of
\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
induces a monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)
, and recall from
[
BPØ22b
, (4.3.4)]
that the functor
\omega^{\log}:X\mapsto X^{\circ}
induces an adjunction

(2.2.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm tr}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\log}^{*}}

where for
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)
,
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F(Y)=F(Y,\textrm{triv})
and for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{lCor}}(k)
,
\omega^{*}_{\log}F(X)=F(\underline{X}-|\partial X|)
. Moreover, since
\omega^{\log}
is monoidal by construction (see
2.1.1
),
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
is monoidal.
We will need later the following immediate result.

Proposition 2.3
.

For all
A\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\tau}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}
and
B\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d\tau}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
, we have that

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d\tau}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(B,\omega^{*}_{\log}A)\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\tau}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}B,A).

2.1.3.
Log motives

In light of Proposition
2.2
, from now until the end of the section, we will consider one of the following situations:

•

\tau
is the Nisnevich topology

•

\tau
is the étale topology and
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra.

Let
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))
be the derived stable
\infty
-category of the Grothendieck abelian category
\mathbf{Shv}_{d\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)
as in
[
Lur17
, Section 1.3.5]
: it is equivalent to the underlying
\infty
-category of the model category
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Cpx}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))
with the
d\tau
-local model structure used in
[
BPØ22b
]
and
[
BM21
]
.

The adjunction
(\omega^{\log}_{\sharp},\omega_{\log}^{*})
of (
2.2.1
) induces the following adjunction of
\infty
-categories of sheaves (see
[
BPØ22b
, 4.3.4]
):

(2.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm tr}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)):R\omega^{*}_{\log}.}

Finally (see
[
BPØ22b
, Section 5.2]
), let
{\overline{\square}}:=(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)
. Notice that
\omega^{\log}({\overline{\square}})=\mathbf{A}^{1}
.

Definition 2.4
.

The stable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\textrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
is the localization of the stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the class of maps

(a_{d\tau}\Lambda({\overline{\square}}\times X))[n]\to(a_{d\tau}\Lambda(X))[n]

for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{lSm}}(k)
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We let

L_{(d\tau,{\overline{\square}})}\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\tau}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))\to\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\textrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)

be the localization functor. For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, we will let
M(X)=L_{d\tau,{\overline{\square}}}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X))
.

The interested reader can verify that this is equivalent to the underlying
\infty
-category of the model category
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Cpx}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))
with the
({\overline{\square}},d\tau)
-local model structure of
[
BPØ22b
, Def. 5.2.1]
and
[
BM21
, Def. 2.9]
. The derived (triangulated) category of effective log motives
\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
is by definition the homotopy category of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
.

We recall the following result, which follows naturally from
[
BM21
, Thm. 5.7]
:

Theorem 2.5
.

The standard
t
-structure of
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)
induces an accessible
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
compatible with filtered colimits in the sense of
[
Lur17
, Def. 1.3.5.20]
, called the
homotopy
t
-structure.

We denote by
\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}
its heart
3
3

3

In
[
BM21
]
, it is denoted by
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
, which is then identified with the category of strictly
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant
d\tau
-sheaves and it is a Grothendieck abelian category. The inclusion

i^{\rm ltr}\colon\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)

admits both a left adjoint
h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}\colon F\mapsto\pi_{0}(L_{(d\tau,{\overline{\square}})}(F[0]))
and a right adjoint
h^{0}_{\mathrm{ltr}}
(see
[
BM21
, Proposition 5.8]
), in particular it is exact and
\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}
inherits a monoidal structure from
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)
given by:

(2.5.1)

F\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}G:=h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}\big(i^{\mathrm{ltr}}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{d\tau}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}i^{\mathrm{ltr}}(G)\big).

2.2.
Comparison with Voevodsky motives

In this subsection, we assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities (see e.g.
[
BPØ22b
, Def. 7.6.3]
for a precise definition). This assumption is always satisfied if
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
.

Definition 2.6
.

Let
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
. A
smooth Cartier compactification
or simply a
Cartier compactification
of
X
is a pair
(\overline{X},D)
where
\overline{X}\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
is proper and
D\subseteq\overline{X}
is an effective Cartier divisor with simple normal crossing such that
\overline{X}-|D|\cong X
.

Note that if
k
admits resolution of singularities, every
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
admits a (smooth) Cartier compactification. This definition is slightly different from the one used in
[
KMSY21a
]
and
[
KMSY21b
]
, where the total space
\overline{X}
is not required to be smooth over
k
, but simply normal. Under our assumption on
k
, this difference is irrelevant.

By
[
BPØ22b
, Prop. 8.2.12]
, the adjunction of (
2.3.1
) descends to an adjunction:

(2.6.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{{\overline{\square}}}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}_{\log},}

where the right-hand side is the
\infty
-category of Voevodsky motives. By
[
BPØ22b
, Thm. 8.2.16 and Thm. 8.2.17]
, the functor
R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}_{\log}
is fully faithful and for
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
and
(\overline{X},D)
a Cartier compactification we have a natural equivalence

R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}_{\log}M(X)\simeq M(\overline{X},\partial X)

with
\partial X
supported on
|D|
. In particular, the essential image of
R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}_{\log}
is the full subcategory spanned by
M(X)
with
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and
\underline{X}
proper. Finally, by
[
BM21
, Prop. 5.12]
it is
t
-exact with respect to the homotopy
t
-structure of
2.5
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
and the Morel-Voevodsky
t
-structure on
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
. In particular, we have a fully faithful functor (still denoted by
\omega^{*}_{\log}
)
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}
between the hearts that commutes with the inclusions. The following result will be crucial in the proof of Proposition
6.10
:

Lemma 2.7
.

The functor
\omega^{*}_{\log}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}
admits a right adjoint (in particular it commutes with all colimits) and is monoidal with respect to the structure (
2.5.1
).

Proof.

By
[
BPØ22b
, Proposition 8.2.12]
, the functor
R^{\overline{\square}}\omega_{\log}^{*}
has a right adjoint
R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{\log}_{*}
, hence since
R^{\overline{\square}}\omega_{\log}^{*}
is
t
-excact by
[
BM21
, Proposition 5.12]
, the functor induced on the hearts are still adjoints by
[
BBD82
, Prop 1.3.17-(iii)]
.
In particular, the functor
\omega^{*}_{\log}
commutes with all colimits, so to conclude it is enough to show that for
X,Y\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
we have

\omega^{*}_{\log}(h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(X\times Y))=\omega^{*}_{\log}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\omega^{*}_{\log}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(Y),

where
h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\tau}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\tau}
is left adjoint to the inclusion (the
0
-th Suslin homology sheaf).
Thanks to
[
BPØ22b
, Proposition 8.2.4]
, for any choice of a smooth Cartier compactification
X\subseteq\overline{X}
and
Y\subseteq\overline{Y}
,
by putting as log structure
\partial X
and
\partial Y
associated with the simple normal crossing divisor
\overline{X}-X
and
\overline{Y}-Y
, we have equivalences in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}
:

R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}_{\log}M^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(X)=M(\overline{X},\partial X)\quad\textrm{and}\quad R^{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}_{\log}M^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(Y)=M(\overline{Y},\partial Y).

By taking
\pi_{0}
,
[
BM21
, Proposition 5.12]
implies that

\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(X)=h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(\overline{X},\partial X)\quad\textrm{and}\quad\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(Y)=h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(\overline{Y},\partial Y).

Hence, we have that

\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(Y)=h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(\overline{X},\partial X)\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(\overline{Y},\partial Y)=h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}((\overline{X},\partial X)\times(\overline{Y},\partial Y)).

Finally, since the underlying scheme of
(\overline{X},\partial X)\times(\overline{Y},\partial Y)
is
\overline{X}\times\overline{Y}
, which is proper, and the subscheme where the log structure is trivial is
X\times Y
, we have that the log scheme
(\overline{X},\partial X)\times(\overline{Y},\partial Y)
is a Cartier compactification of
X\times Y
, hence again by
[
BPØ22b
, Proposition 8.2.4]
we have

h_{0}^{\mathrm{ltr}}((\overline{X},\partial X)\times(\overline{Y},\partial Y))\cong\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(X\times Y),

which concludes the proof.
∎

2.3.
The abelian category of reciprocity sheaves

We recall the construction of the abelian category of reciprocity sheaves via modulus sheaves of
[
KMSY21a
]
and
[
KMSY21b
]
as done in
[
KSY21
]
and some properties.

A pair
\mathfrak{X}=(X,D)
where
X
is a proper scheme of finite type over
k
and
D
is an effective Cartier divisor on
X
is called a
proper modulus pair
if
X-|D|\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
.
Let
\mathfrak{X}=(X,D_{X})
,
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,D_{Y})
be proper modulus pairs and
\Gamma\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X-|D_{X}|,Y-|D_{Y}|)
be a prime correspondence. Let
\overline{\Gamma}\subseteq X\times Y
be the closure of
\Gamma
, and let
\overline{\Gamma}^{N}\to X\times Y
be the normalization. We say that
\Gamma
is
admissible
if
(D_{X})_{\overline{\Gamma}^{N}}\geq(D_{Y})_{\overline{\Gamma}^{N}}
as Weil divisors, where
(E)_{\overline{\Gamma}^{N}}
denotes the pullback of the divisor
E
to the normalization
\overline{\Gamma}^{N}
. By
[
KMSY21a
, Proposition 1.2.7]
, proper modulus pairs and admissible correspondences define an additive category, denoted
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
. For
\mathfrak{X}=(X,D)
and
n\geq 0
, we let
\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}:=(X,nD)
.

We denote by
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
or simply
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
the category of additive presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
, whose objects are called
proper modulus presheaves with trasnfers
. For
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
, we let
\Lambda_{tr}(\mathfrak{X})=\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(-,\mathfrak{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
be the representable object.

Definition 2.8
.

For
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, we let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
be the cofiltered category given by modulus pairs
(\overline{X},D)
given by Cartier compactifications of
X
(see
[
KMSY21a
, Lemma 1.8.2]
and Definition
2.6
).

There is a functor:

\omega:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)\quad(X,D)\mapsto X-|D|,

which induces adjoint functors (cf.
[
KMSY21a
, Pr. 2.2.1]
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{!}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda):\omega^{*}}

where
\omega^{*}
is fully faithful. For
\mathfrak{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
, we have

\omega^{*}F(\mathfrak{X})=F(\omega(\mathfrak{X}))=F(X-|D|).

The functor
\omega_{!}
is given by left Kan extension, so that for
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
and any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
, we have

(2.8.1)

\omega_{!}F(X)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathfrak{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)}F(\mathfrak{Y})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}F(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}).

where the displayed isomorphism follows from
[
Sai20
, Lemma 1.27 (1)]
(with
X_{\infty}^{+}=0
), which implies that we have an isomorphism in
\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
:

``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)})\cong``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"_{\mathfrak{Y}\in\mathbf{Comp}(X)}\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\mathfrak{Y}).

As in the logarithmic case, let
{\overline{\square}}:=(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
and for any
\mathfrak{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
let (see
[
KMSY21a
]
)

\mathfrak{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}:=(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X\times\infty+D\times\mathbf{P}^{1}).

We say
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant if for any
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
, the projection
p:\mathfrak{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}\to\mathfrak{X}
induces an isomorphism

p^{*}:F(\mathfrak{X})\to F(\mathfrak{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}).

We let
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
consisting of all
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant objects. By
[
KSY21
, Lemma 2.1.2]
, it is a Serre subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and that the inclusion functor
i^{\overline{\square}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
has a left adjoint
h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}
and a right adjoint
h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}
given for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
by

\displaystyle h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(F)(\mathfrak{X})=\operatorname{Coker}(i_{0}^{*}-i_{1}^{*}:F(\mathfrak{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}})\to F(\mathfrak{X})),

\displaystyle h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}(F)(\mathfrak{X})=\operatorname{Hom}(h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathfrak{X}),F),

where for
a\in k
the section
i_{a}:\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}
is induced by the map
k[t]\to k[t]/(t-a)\cong k
.
We write
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
for the essential image of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
under
\omega_{!}
. It is an abelian subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
.

Remark 2.9
.

By (
2.8.1
), for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\Lambda)
the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)
,

(ii)

for every
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
and every section
a:\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to F
, there exists
\mathfrak{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
such that
a
factors through
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathfrak{Y})
,

(iii)

for every
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
and every section
a:\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to F
, for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
there exists
n
such that
a
factors through
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)})
.

For
\tau
the Nisnevich or the étale topology, we let
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda):=\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
.
The objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
) are called reciprocity presheaves (resp.
\tau
-reciprocity sheaves) of
\Lambda
-modules.
By
[
Sai20
, Thm. 0.1]
, the Nisnevich sheafification restricts to a functor

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{V}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda),

which makes
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
a Grothendieck abelian category (see
[
KSY21
, Corollary 2.4.2]
). Notice in particular that
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
is closed under sub-objects and quotients in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
, and that the inclusion functor
i\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
is exact. As in
[
KSY21
, Theorem 2.4.3 (1)]
, we denote by
\rho
the right adjoint to the inclusion
i
.

By
[
Sai20
, Theorem 0.2]
, each
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
satisfies
global injectivity
, i.e. for every
X\in\mathbf{Sm}
connected with generic point
\eta_{X}
, the restriction map gives an injective map:

F(X)\hookrightarrow F(\eta_{X}).

By Proposition
2.1
, if
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra the étale sheafification coincides with the Nisnevich sheafification, hence it restricts to a functor

(2.9.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in particular, if
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra,
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)
is a Grothendieck abelian category and every
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)
satisfies global injectivity.

We have two important examples of reciprocity sheaves:

(1)

Let
\mathcal{G}^{*}
be the category of smooth commutative
k
-group schemes (i.e.
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
is a group scheme such that the connected component of the identity
G^{0}
is a smooth commutative algebraic group and
\pi_{0}(G)
is finitely generated, see
[
ABV09
, 1.3]
for an analogous definition on semi-abelian group schemes). It is classical that the corresponding étale sheaf has a unique structure of sheaf with transfers (see
[
SS03
, Lemma 3.2]
) and by
[
KSYR16
, Theorem 4.4]
it has reciprocity in the sense of
[
KSYR16
, Definition 2.1.3]
.
This defines a functor
\mathcal{G}^{*}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
, generalizing the functor
\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\rm sab}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
considered in
[
BVK16
]
.

(2)

For
\mathfrak{C}=(C,C_{\infty})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
with
C
geometrically connected and
\dim(C)=1
, let
\underline{\operatorname{Pic}}(C,C_{\infty})
denote the relative Picard group scheme. We would like to underline that
C_{\infty}
is not supposed to be reduced. By
[
RY16
, Thm. 1.1]
combined with
[
KSY21
, Lem 2.2.2]
, we have that

(2.9.2)

\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathfrak{C}))=\underline{\operatorname{Pic}}(C,C_{\infty}).

We end this subsection recalling the following result:

Proposition 2.10
.

If
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, the “forgetting transfers” functor
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)
is fully faithful and exact.

Proof.

The argument for homotopy invariant sheaves with transfers given in
[
BVK16
, 3.9]
works here as well, replacing the reference to Voevodsky’s purity theorem for homotopy invariant sheaves with the global injectivity provided by
[
Sai20
, Theorem 0.2]
.∎

2.4.
Reciprocity sheaves and logarithmic motives

In this subsection, we continue to assume that
k
satisfies resolution of singularities. Let
\tau
and
\Lambda
be as in
2.1.3
.

By
[
Sai21
]
, there exists a fully faithful and exact functor

\mathcal{L}og:\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)\to\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(k,\Lambda)

such that
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\mathcal{L}og\simeq id
.
If
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, by Proposition
2.2
, we have similarly a fully faithful and exact functor:

(2.10.1)

\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}_{\log}:\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)\overset{\eqref{eq;et-Nis-RSC}}{\cong}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)\overset{\mathcal{L}og}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(k,\Lambda)\cong\mathbf{logCI}_{\textrm{d\'{e}t}}(k,\Lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{\textrm{d\'{e}t}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda).

Remark 2.11
.

The functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}
commutes with all colimits: to see this, let
\{F_{i}\}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
be an inductive system, then we have a natural map

(2.11.1)

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(F_{i})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}F_{i})

Since
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}(k,\Lambda)\colon\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
is faithful, hence conservative (this follows from the results of
[
BM21
]
, see
[
BM22
, Prop. 0.1]
), it is enough to show that (
2.11.1
) is an isomorphism after applying
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
. We have that

\displaystyle\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(F_{i})

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\cong}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(F_{i})\overset{(*2)}{\cong}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}F_{i}\overset{(*3)}{\cong}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}F_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{(*4)}{\cong}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}F_{i})

where
(*1)
comes from the fact that
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
has a right adjoint, so it preserves colimits,
(*2)
and
(*4)
follow from the fact that
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}=id_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}
,
(*3)
from the fact that the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\Lambda)
has a right adjoint, so it preserves colimits. In particular, this implies that the composition (
2.10.1
) preserves all colimits.

Remark 2.12
.

Let
F\in\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)
such that
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
, we have that

\omega^{*}_{\log}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F\cong\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F)\in\mathbf{logCI}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda),

hence we have that the natural map

\eta\colon F\to\omega^{*}_{\log}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F
is a map in
\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)
such that
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}(\eta)
is an isomorphism. Since
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
is faithful and exact, it is conservative. We conclude that:

(2.12.1)

F\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F\cong\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F).

Notice that (
2.12.1
) strictly depends on the fact that
\omega^{*}_{\log}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F\in\mathbf{logCI}_{d\tau}
, which is not true unless
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\tau}
.

Remark 2.13
.

Recall that the functor
\omega^{\log}
from (
2.2.1
) admits a left adjoint
\lambda^{\log}\colon\mathbf{Sm}(k)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
such that

\lambda^{\log}(Y)=(Y,\mathrm{triv})
for
Y\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
,
which itself has a left adjoint which associates the underlying scheme
{\underline{X}}
to
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
. Moreover, these functors preserve transfers.
In particular, we have a left exact functor

\lambda_{\sharp}^{\log}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Z})

such that
\lambda^{\log}_{\sharp}F(X)=F({\underline{X}})
for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and it is a left adjoint to
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
.
Moreover, as observed in
[
BPØ22b
, 4.3, p.64]
, the functor
\lambda_{\sharp}^{\log}
sends
\tau
-sheaves to
d\tau
-sheaves, which implies that we have an adjunction:

(2.13.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm tr}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}}

where
\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}
is the restriction of
\lambda_{\sharp}^{\log}
under
the forgetful functor
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(k,\Lambda)
.
Since the latter functor commutes with limits as a right adjoint, both
\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}
and
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
are exact, so they derive trivially.
Hence, (
2.13.1
) induces the following adjunction of
\infty
-categories of sheaves:

(2.13.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\omega^{\sharp}_{\log})\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm tr}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\rm ltr}_{d\tau}(k,\Lambda)):\mathcal{D}(\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}).}

We remark that by the construction of
\mathcal{L}og
in
[
Sai21
, §6]
and

[
RS21
, Cor. 6.8(1)]
, we have

(2.13.3)

\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}\mathbf{G}_{a}(X)=\Gamma({\underline{X}},\mathcal{O}_{X})=\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{a}(X).

3.
Categories of rational maps and universal problems

3.1.
Commutative groups schemes and torsors under them

We recall some well-known facts on commutative group schemes over a perfect field
k
and we fix some notation.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{*}
be again the category of smooth commutative
k
-group schemes, locally of finite
type over
k
and such that
\pi_{0}(G)
is finitely generated (for short, a commutative
k
-group scheme). Write
\mathcal{G}
for the subcategory of smooth commutative algebraic
k
-groups (i.e. objects of
\mathcal{G}^{*}
which are of finite type). Given
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
, let
G^{0}
be the connected component of the identity in
G
.
Recall (see
[
BVK16
, Definition 1.1.2]
or
[
DG80
, Proposition 5.1.4]
) the following definition.

Definition 3.1
.

A group scheme
L\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
is called
discrete
if
L^{0}=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
and the abelian group
L(\overline{k})
is finitely generated (equivalently, if
L
is étale over
k
). A discrete
k
-group scheme
L\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
is called a
lattice
if
L(\overline{k})
is torsion free.

As in
[
BVK16
]
, we denote by
{}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{0}
the subcategory of
\mathcal{G}^{*}
consisting of discrete
k
-group schemes. By
[
BVK16
, Lemma 1.1.3]
it is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{G}^{*}
, hence it is an Abelian category. We denote by
\mathcal{M}_{0}
the full subcategory of lattices.
By
[
DG80
, Proposition 5.1.8]
, for
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
, there is an exact sequence

0\to G^{0}\to G\to\pi_{0}(G)\to 0

where
\pi_{0}(G)
is an étale
k
-group, which is universal for homomorphisms from
G
to discrete groups. The fibers of
G\to\pi_{0}(G)
are the irreducible components of
G
.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
G\in\mathcal{G}
be a smooth commutative algebraic
k
-group. By
k
-torsor under
G
or for
G
we mean a
k
-scheme
P
, locally of finite type over
k
, equipped with an action
P\times G\to P
such that the induced morphism
(s,g)\mapsto(s,sg)\colon P\times G\to P\times P
is an isomorphism. Write
\mathcal{P}_{G}
for the category of
k
-torsors under
G
: morphisms between torsors are
G
-equivariant
k
-morphisms.

3.1.1.

Given a
k
-torsor
P
under
G\in\mathcal{G}
, we can construct a commutative
k
-group scheme
P_{G}=\coprod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\vee n}\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
using the sum of torsors
\vee_{G}
(see
[
Mil80
, III.4.8.b]
) following
[
Ram01
, 1.2]
. It fits in a short exact sequence

0\to G\to P_{G}\xrightarrow{a_{P}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\to 0,

presenting
P_{G}
as extension of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
by the group
G
. Moreover, we can identify the torsor
P
with the fiber of the section
1\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
along the map
a_{P}
, so that we have a natural inclusion
P\hookrightarrow P_{G}
.

3.1.2.

Write
\mathcal{P}
for the category whose objects are pairs
(P,G)
, where
P\in\mathcal{P}_{G}
for a
G\in\mathcal{G}
a smooth commutative connected algebraic group. A morphism in
\mathcal{P}
is the datum of a pair of morphisms
(f^{1},f^{0})\colon(P,G)\to(P^{\prime},G^{\prime})
, where
f^{0}\colon G\to G^{\prime}
is a
k
-morphism of algebraic groups and
f^{1}\colon P\to P^{\prime}
is
f^{0}
-equivariant. If
X
is a
k
-scheme, we write
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
for the comma category over
X
: its objects are triples
(u,P,G)
, where
(P,G)\in\mathcal{P}
and
u\colon X\to P
is a
k
-morphisms. Morphisms in
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
are defined in the obvious way.

Definition 3.3
.

A
fibration to torsors
is the datum, for each
X\in\mathbf{Sm}
, of a full category
\mathcal{M}_{X}
of
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
, contravariantly functorial in
X
.
Similarly, a fibration to torsors
for proper modulus pairs
is the datum, for each
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},X_{\infty})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, of a full subcategory
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{X}}
of
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
, where
X=\overline{X}\setminus X_{\infty}\in\mathbf{Sm}
, contravariantly functorial in
\mathfrak{X}
for maps in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MSm}}^{\rm fin}
(see
[
KMSY21a
, Definition 1.3.3.(2)]
).
The initial object (if it exists) of
\mathcal{M}_{X}
is called the
\mathcal{M}
-Albanese torsor of
X
. By definition, it is the datum of a smooth commutative connected algebraic group
\operatorname{Alb}^{0}_{\mathcal{M}}(X)
, a
k
-torsor
\operatorname{Alb}^{1}_{\mathcal{M}}(X)
under
\operatorname{Alb}^{0}_{\mathcal{M}}(X)
and a
k
-morphism
X\to\operatorname{Alb}^{1}_{\mathcal{M}}(X)
which is universal for maps in
\mathcal{M}_{X}
. The algebraic group
\operatorname{Alb}^{0}_{\mathcal{M}}(X)
is called the
\mathcal{M}
-Albanese variety of
X
. Similarly, if
\mathcal{M}_{-}
is a fibration to torsors for proper modulus pairs, the initial object (if it exists) of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is called the
\mathcal{M}
-Albanese torsor of
\mathfrak{X}
. The corresponding algebraic group,
\operatorname{Alb}^{0}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{X})
will be called the
\mathcal{M}
-Albanese variety of
\mathfrak{X}
.

Example 3.4
.

For any
X
, let
\mathbf{SAb}_{X}
be the full subcategory of
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
consisting of maps to torsors
P
under semi-Abelian varieties. In this case, the existence of an initial object for
\mathbf{SAb}_{X}
was proven by Serre
[
Ser60
]
in the case the base field
k
is algebraically closed. In
[
Wit08
, Appendix A]
, a Galois descent argument is used to show that the Albanese variety, the Albanese torsor and the universal map

X\to\operatorname{Alb}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{SAb}}(X)

always exist, without any assumption on
k
.

If
X
is smooth and proper over
k
, the semi-abelian variety
\operatorname{Alb}^{0}_{\mathbf{SAb}}(X)
is in fact an Abelian variety, and coincides with the classical Albanese variety of
X
, dual (as abelian variety) to the Picard variety
\mathrm{Pic}_{X}^{0,red}
.

3.2.
A universal construction

We will discuss a number of situations in which the
\mathcal{M}
-Albanese torsor of a proper modulus pair
\mathfrak{X}
exists. In fact, we will consider different fibrations to torsors
\mathcal{M}_{-}
for proper modulus pairs, giving sufficient conditions for the initial object to exist. In the end, all the fibrations that we consider will turn out to be equivalent, giving then a
unique notion of Albanese torsor
for a proper modulus pair
\mathfrak{X}
. We recall the following result due to Serre
[
Ser60
]
:

Theorem 3.5
.

Let
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
and
\mathcal{C}
be a subcategory of
X\backslash\mathcal{G}
satisfying the following conditions:

\mathrm{(I)}

If
u_{i}:X\to G_{i}
are in
\mathcal{C}
for
i=1,2
, then
u_{1}\times u_{2}:X\to G_{1}\times G_{2}
is in
\mathcal{C}
.

\mathrm{(II)}

For a homomorphism
f:H\to G
in
\mathcal{G}
such that
\operatorname{Ker}(f)
is finite and

v:X\to H
such that
u={f\circ v}:X\to G
is in
\mathcal{C}
,
v:X\to H
is in
\mathcal{C}
.

Then:

(1)

A morphism
u:X\to G
in
X\backslash\mathcal{G}
is universal if and only if it is maximal in the sense of [Def.2, Ser60] and for any maximal morphism
v:X\to H
in
\mathcal{C}
, we have

\dim H\leq\dim G
.

(2)

There exists a universal object in
\mathcal{C}
if and only if there exists
N>0
such that
for any maximal morphism
u:X\to G
in
\mathcal{C}
, we have
\dim(G)\leq N
.

Definition 3.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
be a proper geometrically integral modulus pair, and write
U(\mathfrak{X})=U(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
for the
k
-vector space
H^{0}(\overline{X},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X},cl}(X_{\infty}))
, where
\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X},cl}(X_{\infty}):=\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X},cl}\cap\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}}(X_{\infty})
denotes the subsheaf of closed forms.
Let
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
be the full subcategory of
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
consisting of triples
(u,P,G)
with the following property: Let
k\subset\overline{k}
be an algebraic closure of
k
and let
u_{\overline{k}}\colon{X}_{\overline{k}}\to P_{\overline{k}}\cong G_{\overline{k}}
be the base change of
u
to
\overline{k}
.
Then
(u,P,G)\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
if and only if
(u_{\overline{k}})^{*}\Omega(G_{\overline{k}})\subseteq U(\overline{X}_{\overline{k}},X_{\infty,\overline{k}})
, where
\Omega(G_{\overline{k}})
denotes the space of invariant differential forms on
G_{\overline{k}}
. The assignment
\mathfrak{X}\mapsto\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
defines a fibration to torsors for proper modulus pairs in the sense of Definition
3.3

Remark 3.7
.

It follows immediately that
(u,P,G)\in X\backslash\mathcal{P}
belongs to
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
if and only if

u_{L}^{*}\Omega(G_{L})\subseteq U(\overline{X}_{L},X_{\infty,L})

for any algebraically closed field
L\subset k
. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
satisfies condition (I) of Theorem
3.5
and, if
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, it satisfies (II) too since
\Omega(G_{\overline{k}})\to\Omega(H_{\overline{k}})
is surjective for
f:H\to G
as in (II).

Theorem 3.8
.

If
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, then for any
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, the
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-Albanese torsor of
\mathfrak{X}
exists.

Proof.

Suppose that
k=\overline{k}
is algebraically closed. Since any
k
-torsor under an algebraic group
G\in\mathcal{G}
is trivial in this case, the category
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is equivalent to the category of morphisms
u:X\to G
in
X\backslash\mathcal{G}
satisfying the condition
u^{*}(\Omega(G))\subseteq U(\mathfrak{X})
. Morphisms in
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
are
k
-morphisms
f\colon G\to G^{\prime}
of
torsors
commuting with the structural morphisms
u\colon X\to G
and
u^{\prime}\colon X\to G^{\prime}
(where we view
G
and
G^{\prime}
acting on themselves). Although the morphism
f
is not a homomorphism of algebraic groups in general, it can be written as
f=f_{0}+\tau
, where
f_{0}
is a group homomorphism and
\tau
is a translation.
Following
[
Ser60
]
, in order to check whether an initial object for
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
exists using Theorem
3.5
, it is enough to restrict to the subcategory
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega,g}_{\mathfrak{X}}
of morphisms which are
generating
(see
[
Ser60
, Definition 1]
).
For them, one has the following simple

Lemma 3.9

(Lemma 6, p.198,
[
FW84
]
)
.

Let
u\colon X\to G\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
and suppose that
u
is generating in the sense of
[
Ser60
, Definition 1]
. Then the pullback map

u^{*}\colon H^{0}(G,\Omega^{1}_{G})^{\rm inv}=\Omega(G)\to H^{0}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})

is injective.

By e.g.
[
Ser75
, Prop. III.16]
, the dimension of any
G\in\mathcal{G}
agrees with the dimension of the
k
-vector space
\Omega(G)
of invariant differential forms. The previous Lemma implies that for any
u\colon X\to G\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega,g}_{\mathfrak{X}}
, one has
\dim G\leq\dim U(\mathfrak{X})
.
By Theorem
3.5
, this concludes the case
k=\overline{k}
.

Suppose now that
k
is any perfect field and let
\overline{k}
be an algebraic closure of
k
. Write
\overline{X}_{\overline{k}}
for the base change
\overline{X}\otimes_{k}\overline{k}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{k}}
for the pair
(\overline{X}_{\overline{k}},(X_{\infty})_{\overline{k}})
. According to the above argument, the category
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{k}}}
admits a universal object,

{\rm alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{k}}}\colon X_{\overline{k}}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{k}}}.

The descent to the base field
k
can be done following the proof of Serre
[
Ser75
, V.22]
in the case of generalized Jacobians of curves to get a triple
({\rm alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\Omega},\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}},\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}})
defined over
k
.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

It should be possible to closely follow the construction of
[
ESV99
]
of the universal regular quotient of the Chow group of zero cycles, that works over any field, to remove the hypothesis on the characteristic of
k
. This was used in
[
BK18
]
to construct the universal regular quotient of the Kerz-Saito Chow group of zero cycles with modulus. Since the applications we have in mind in the later sections will require the characteristic zero assumption, we have decided to not pursue this goal here.

3.3.
Cutting curves

Assume now that
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},X_{\infty})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
is such that
\overline{X}
is smooth over
k
. A finite morphism
\nu\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}
, with
\overline{C}
a normal and geometrically integral curve, is
admissible
for
\mathfrak{X}
if
\nu(\overline{C})\not\subseteq X_{\infty}
. In this case, write
C_{\infty}
for the effective Cartier divisor
\nu^{*}X_{\infty}
on
\overline{C}
. Write
C
for the open subset
\overline{C}\setminus C_{\infty}
. We will use the following Lemma, taken from
[
BS19
]
.

Lemma 3.11

(Lemma 10.14,
[
BS19
]
)
.

Let
\gamma
be the restriction map

\gamma\colon H^{0}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\to\prod_{\nu\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}}H^{0}(C,\Omega^{1}_{C})/H^{0}(\overline{C},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{C}}(\nu^{*}X_{\infty})),

where the product runs over the set of admissible curves
\nu\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}
. Then the kernel of
\gamma
agrees with
H^{0}(\overline{X},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}}(X_{\infty}))
.

If
u\colon X\to P
is a
k
-morphism from
X
to a torsor
P
for an algebraic group
G\in\mathcal{G}
, we get by composition a morphism

u_{C}\colon C:=\overline{C}\times_{\overline{X}}X\to X\xrightarrow{u}P.

Write
\nu^{*}(u,P,G)
for the corresponding object in
C\backslash\mathcal{P}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

A triple
(u,P,G)\in X\backslash\mathcal{P}
belongs to
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
if and only if for any admissible curve
\nu\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}
, we have
\nu^{*}(u,P,G)\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{(\overline{C},C_{\infty})}
.

Proof.

The necessity of the condition is clear. According to Definition
3.6
and Remark
3.7
, the statement of the Lemma can be checked over an algebraic closure
\overline{k}
of
k
, so that we can assume
k=\overline{k}
. As above, the category
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is equivalent to the category of morphisms
\psi\colon X\to G
from
X
to algebraic groups satisfying the condition
\psi^{*}(\Omega(G))\subseteq U(\mathfrak{X})
.
Let now
\psi\colon X\to G
be a
k
-morphism, and let
\omega\in\Omega(G)
. We have to show that
\psi^{*}(\omega)\in H^{0}(\overline{X},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}}(X_{\infty}))
(note that
\psi^{*}(\omega)
is automatically closed), i.e. that
\eta:=\psi^{*}(\omega)
has poles along
|X_{\infty}|
of order bounded by the multiplicity of
X_{\infty}
, assuming that this condition is satisfied after restriction admissible to curves. But this is precisely the content of Lemma
3.11
.
∎

3.4.
The universal regular quotient of the Chow group of zero cycles

We start by recalling the definition of the Kerz-Saito Chow group of
0
-cycles with modulus (see
[
KS16
]
).
For an integral scheme
\overline{C}
over
k
and for
E
a closed subscheme of
\overline{C}
, we set

\displaystyle G(\overline{C},E)

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{x\in E}\mathrm{Ker}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C},x}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{E,x}^{\times}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{E\subset U\subset\overline{C}}\Gamma(U,\mathrm{ker}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times})),

where
U
runs over the set of open subsets of
\overline{C}
containing
E
(the intersection taking place in the function field
k(\overline{C})^{\times}
). We say that a rational function
f\in G(\overline{C},E)
satisfies the modulus condition with respect to
E
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},X_{\infty})\in{\bf MCor}
be a proper modulus pair and write
X
for the complement
\overline{X}\setminus{|X_{\infty}|}
. Let
Z_{0}(X)
be the free abelian group on the set of closed points of
X
. Let
\overline{C}
be an integral normal curve over
k
and
let
\varphi_{\overline{C}}\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}
