be a finite morphism such that
\varphi_{\overline{C}}(\overline{C})\not\subset X_{\infty}
(so
\overline{C}
is admissible in the sense of
3.3
). The push forward of cycles along the restriction of
\varphi_{\overline{C}}
to
C=\overline{C}\times_{\overline{X}}X
gives a well defined group homomorphism

\tau_{\overline{C}}\colon G(\overline{C},\varphi_{\overline{C}}^{*}(X_{\infty}))\to Z_{0}(X),

sending a function
f
to the push forward of the divisor
{\rm div}_{\overline{C}}(f)
.

Definition 3.13

(Kerz-Saito)
.

We define the Chow group
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})={\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\overline{X}|X_{\infty})
of 0-cycles of
\overline{X}
with modulus
X_{\infty}
as the cokernel of the homomorphism

(3.13.1)

\tau\colon G(\overline{X},X_{\infty})\to Z_{0}(X)\;\text{ with }G(\overline{X},X_{\infty})=\bigoplus_{\varphi_{\overline{C}}\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}}G(\overline{C},\varphi_{\overline{C}}^{*}(X_{\infty}))

where the sum runs over the set of finite morphisms
\varphi_{\overline{C}}\colon\overline{C}\to X
from admissible curves.

Remark 3.14
.

Let
\pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{0}(X)\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})
. If
\dim(X)=1
, then by construction the map
\tau
is injective, so
G(\overline{X},X_{\infty})=\operatorname{Ker}(\pi_{\mathfrak{X}})
and
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})\simeq\operatorname{Pic}(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
. In particular, if
X_{\infty}^{\prime}\geq X_{\infty}
we have an isomorphism

(3.14.1)

\frac{G(\overline{X},X^{\prime}_{\infty})}{G(\overline{X},X_{\infty})}\cong\mathrm{ker}(\operatorname{Pic}(X,X^{\prime}_{\infty})\to\operatorname{Pic}(X,X_{\infty}))

Definition 3.15
.

Let
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
geometrically connected and
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
(see Definition
2.8
). Let
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})
be the Chow group of
0
-cycles with modulus of
\mathfrak{X}
. Let
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
be the full subcategory of
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
consisting of triples
(u,P,G)
with the following property. For any algebraically closed field
L\supset k
, write
u_{L}\colon Z_{0}(X)^{0}\to P_{L}(L)\cong G_{L}(L)
for the induced morphism on zero-cycles of degree zero. Then
(u,P,G)\in\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
if and only if
u_{L}
factors as

\textstyle{Z_{0}(X_{L})^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{L}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{L})^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{L}(L),}

where
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{L})^{0}
is the image of
Z_{0}(X_{L})^{0}
.

Remark 3.16
.

Since
G
is a reciprocity sheaf by
[
KSY21
, Cor.3.2.5]
, for
u\in G(X)
there always exists a modulus
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
such that the induced map
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to G
factors through
h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})=\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathfrak{X})
(cf. Remark
2.9
(ii)).
By taking sections over an algebraically closed field
L\supset k
we get that
u_{L}
factors as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)(L)\cong Z_{0}(X_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})(L)\underset{(*)}{\cong}{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{L}(L),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{L}}

where the isomorphism
(*)
follows from
[
KSY21
, Remark 2.2.3]
.
In particular, for all
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
, the diagram above factors further through the map
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{L})\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{L})
.

Lemma 3.17
.

If
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, then
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
satisfies the conditions
\mathrm{(I)}
and
\mathrm{(II)}
of Theorem
3.5
.

Proof.

The assertion is obvious for (I). We prove it for (II).
Take a homomorphism
f:H\to G
in
\mathcal{G}
such that
\operatorname{Ker}(f)
is finite and

v:X\to H
such that
u=f\cdot v:X\to G
is in
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
We want to prove
v:X\to H
is in
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
, equivalently

\operatorname{Ker}(\pi_{L,\mathfrak{X}}:Z_{0}(X_{L})^{0}\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{L})^{0})\subset\operatorname{Ker}(v_{L}:Z_{0}(X_{L})^{0}\to H_{L}(L))

for every algebraically closed field
L\supset k
.
Since
H
is a reciprocity sheaf, by Remark
3.16
there exists
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}=(\overline{X},X^{\prime}_{\infty})
such that
v_{L}
factors through
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{L})^{0}
, so
\operatorname{Ker}(\pi_{L,\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}})\subset\operatorname{Ker}(v_{L})
. Since we can always factor through maps
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
as observed in Remark
3.16
, we can choose
X^{\prime}_{\infty}\geq X_{\infty}
.
This implies that
v_{L}
induces a map

\overline{v}_{L}:\frac{G(\overline{X}_{L},(X_{\infty})_{L})}{G(\overline{X}_{L},(X^{\prime}_{\infty})_{L})}\to H(L),

where the left hand side is equal to

\bigoplus_{\varphi_{\overline{C}}\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}_{L}}\frac{G(\overline{C},C_{\infty})}{G(\overline{C},C^{\prime}_{\infty})}\;\text{ with }C_{\infty}=\varphi_{\overline{C}}^{*}((X_{\infty})_{L}),\;C^{\prime}_{\infty}=\varphi_{\overline{C}}^{*}((X^{\prime}_{\infty})_{L}),

where
\varphi_{\overline{C}}\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}_{L}
are as in (
3.13.1
) with
\overline{X}
replaced by
\overline{X}_{L}
.
Moreover, the image of
\overline{v}_{L}
lies in
\operatorname{Ker}(f)(L)
, which is finite by the assumption. We will show that the group

\frac{G(\overline{C},C_{\infty})}{G(\overline{C},C^{\prime}_{\infty})}
is divisible, which will imply that the map
\overline{v}_{L}
is the zero map. As observed in Remark
3.14
, we have

\frac{G(\overline{C},C_{\infty})}{G(\overline{C},C^{\prime}_{\infty}))}=\operatorname{Ker}(\operatorname{Pic}(\overline{C},C^{\prime}_{\infty}))\to\operatorname{Pic}(\overline{C}_{L},C_{\infty})),

and the latter equals to
(L_{+})^{r}\times(L^{\times})^{s}
, where
r,s
are some non-negative integers. In particular, the above group is divisible thanks to the assumption
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, so
\overline{u}_{L}
is the zero map.
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Assume
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
and that
\mathfrak{X}
is a smooth and proper modulus pair over
k
. Then the
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-Albanese torsor
({\rm alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}},\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}},\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}})
of
\mathfrak{X}
exists. If
\dim\mathfrak{X}=1
, it agrees with the Rosenlich-Serre generalized Jacobian
(\varphi_{X_{\infty}},{\operatorname{Jac}}_{(\overline{X},X_{\infty})}^{(1)},{\operatorname{Jac}}_{(\overline{X},X_{\infty})}^{(0)})
of
[
Ser75
, V.4.20]
.

Proof.

If
\dim\mathfrak{X}=1
, this is precisely the content of
[
Ser75
, V.Theorem 1]
, and the very definition of modulus for a rational map and local symbols. For the general case, as in the proof of Theorem
3.8
, it is enough to show the existence in the case
k=\overline{k}
is algebraically closed (the descent argument is identical). Similarly, we can restrict to the category
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}},g}_{\mathfrak{X}}
of morphisms which are generating. Then by Theorem
3.5
and Lemma
3.17
, it is then enough to show that there exists a uniform bound on the dimensions of the groups appearing in
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}},g}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. We do this by showing that
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a (full) subcategory of
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. The required bound will be then provided by Lemma
3.9
.

Suppose then that that
u\colon X\to G
is a
k
-morphism from
X=\overline{X}\setminus|X_{\infty}|
to a commutative connected algebraic group
G
such that the induced map on zero cycles factors through
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})
. Let
\varphi\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}
be a finite morphism from normal integral curve
\overline{C}
such that
\varphi(\overline{C})\not\subset X_{\infty}
. Put
C=\overline{C}\times_{\overline{X}}X
and
C_{\infty}=\varphi^{*}(X_{\infty})
. Let
u_{C}\colon C\to G
be the composition
u\circ\varphi
. Then we have

u_{C}(\operatorname{div}_{\overline{C}}(f))=u(\varphi_{*}(\operatorname{div}_{\overline{C}}(f)))=0\text{ in }G(k)\text{ for any }f\in G(\overline{C},C_{\infty}).

In particular, the divisor
C_{\infty}
is a modulus in the sense of Rosenlicht-Serre for the rational map (still denoted
u_{C}
)
u_{C}\colon\overline{C}\dashrightarrow G
. Therefore we have then a factorization (
[
Ser75
, V, Theorem 2]
)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.93945pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.93124pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 24.25157pt\raise-38.69443pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{u}_{C}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.93945pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.93945pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
a\colon C\to{\operatorname{Jac}}_{\overline{C},C_{\infty}}
is the universal map from
C
to its generalized Jacobian (with respect to a chosen
k
-rational point). But now we have

u_{C}^{*}(\Omega(G))=a^{*}(\tilde{u}_{C}^{*}(\Omega(G)))\subseteq a^{*}(\Omega({\operatorname{Jac}}_{\overline{C},C_{\infty}}))=H^{0}(\overline{C},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{C}}\otimes_{\overline{C}}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}(C_{\infty})),

where the last equality follows from
[
Ser75
, V, Proposition 5]
. This implies that
(u_{C},G)\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{(\overline{C},C_{\infty})}
for any admissible curve and so, by Lemma
3.12
, we deduce that
(u,G)\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
∎

3.4.1.

Assume
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
. Let
\mathfrak{X}
be as above. From the proof of Proposition
3.18
, we deduce immediately the existence of a natural surjective map of torsors

\rho^{\Omega,{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}

equivariant with respect to a surjective homomorphism of algebraic
k
groups
\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}

such that
{\rm alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}=\rho^{\Omega,{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ{\rm alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. We will see below that those maps are isomorphisms.
To further relate the Chow groups of zero cycles with modulus of a pair
\mathfrak{X}
with the
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
Albanese construction of
3.2
, we also recall the following

Proposition 3.19

(
[
Ser75
, III, Proposition 10]
or
[
KSYR16
, Proposition 4.3.1]
)
.

Let
G
be a commutative algebraic group over a field
K
of characteristic zero. Let
\overline{C}
be a proper normal curve over
K
,
C
an open dense subscheme of
\overline{C}
and
\psi\colon C\to G
a
K
-morphism. Let
D
be an effective divisor on
\overline{C}
supported on
\overline{C}\setminus C
such that

\psi^{*}(\Omega(G))\subset H^{0}(\overline{C},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{C}}\otimes_{\overline{C}}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}(D)).

Then we have
\operatorname{div}_{\overline{C}}(f)^{*}\psi=0
in
G(K)
for any

f\in G(\overline{C},D)
.

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero. Then the
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-Albanese torsor of
\mathfrak{X}
agrees with the
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-Albanese torsor
({\rm alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\Omega},\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}},\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}})
of Theorem
3.8
.

Proof.

It is enough to show that the two categories
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
have the same objects (as they are both full subcategories of
X\backslash\mathcal{P}
). According to Definitions
3.15
and
3.6
, it is enough to show the statement under the assumption that
k=\overline{k}
is algebraically closed.
We already know thanks to the proof of Proposition
3.18
that
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a full subcategory of
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}

(this does not require
k
to be of characteristic
0
).
To prove the other inclusion, let
u\colon X\to G\in\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. By functoriality, for any
\mathfrak{X}
-admissible morphism
\varphi\colon\overline{C}\to\overline{X}
from a normal integral curve, the composition
u_{C}\colon\overline{C}\times_{\overline{X}}X\to X\to G
satisfies
u^{*}_{C}(\Omega(G))\subseteq H^{0}(\overline{C},\Omega^{1}_{\overline{C}}\otimes_{\overline{C}}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}(C_{\infty}))
, where
C_{\infty}
denotes as before the pullback
\varphi^{*}(X_{\infty})
. By Proposition
3.19
above, we have
u_{C}(\operatorname{div}_{\overline{C}}(f))=u(\varphi_{*}\operatorname{div}_{\overline{C}}(f))=0
in
G(k)
for any
f\in G(\overline{C},C_{\infty})
, so that the map induced by
u
on the group of zero cycles of degree zero
Z_{0}(X)^{0}
factors through
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})^{0}
. The same argument applies to any base-change to
L\supset k
algebraically closed, so that
(u,G)\in\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
as required.
∎

Remark 3.21
.

Suppose
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
and that
k=\overline{k}
is algebraically closed and let
\mathfrak{X}
be a smooth proper integral modulus pair. Then the morphism

\rho_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})^{0}\to\operatorname{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}(k)

to the Albanese variety
\operatorname{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}=\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
of
\mathfrak{X}
induced by
{\rm alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is surjective and regular. We can reformulate the universal property in
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
by saying that
\operatorname{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is the universal regular quotient of the Chow group of zero cycles with modulus. As such it agrees, a posteriori, with the Albanese variety
\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}|X_{\infty})
of
[
BK18
, Theorem 1.1]
. If
k=\mathbb{C}
and
|X_{\infty}|
is a strict normal crossing divisor, it agrees with the generalized Jacobian
J^{d}_{\overline{X}|X_{\infty}}
(for
d=\dim{\overline{X}}
) studied in
[
BS19
, 10.2]
. Despite the fact that it is defined starting from a different modulus condition on algebraic cycles, it agrees also with the Albanese variety with modulus
\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
defined by Russell
[
Rus13
]
. This is a consequence of Lemma
3.12
and the fact that both
\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
and
\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}(\mathfrak{X})
agree with the Rosenlicht-Serre generalized Jacobian in the one-dimensional case
4
4

4

An independent (and explicit) proof of the fact that over
\mathbb{C}
the generalized Jacobian
J^{d}_{\overline{X}|X_{\infty}}
agrees with
\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
defined by Russell has been given by T. Yamazaki
[
Yam17
]
, using Hodge-theoretic methods.
.

3.5.
The Albanese scheme with modulus

Assume
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
.
We can now extend the construction of Ramachandran
[
Ram01
]
to the modulus setting. Let
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
be geometrically integral and
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
. Thanks to Theorem
3.8
, we have a map, defined over
k
,

(3.21.1)

{\rm alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\Omega,(1)}\colon X\to\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}

universal for morphisms from
X
to torsors under commutative algebraic groups in
\mathcal{M}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
(in the following, we shall say “to torsors under commutative algebraic groups with modulus
\mathfrak{X}
”). Let
\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
be the
k
-group scheme
\coprod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}})^{\otimes n}
constructed in
3.1.1
. The universal map (
3.21.1
) composed with the natural inclusion of
\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
in
\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
gives then a canonical morphism

a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon X\to\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}

which is now universal for morphisms to
k
-group schemes in the appropriate sense. By construction, the
k
-group scheme
\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is an extension

(3.21.2)

0\to\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\to 0.

If
X
has a
k
-rational point, the extension is split, i.e. we have an isomorphism
\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}\cong\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}\times\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
. This happens in particular when
k
is algebraically closed, and corresponds to the fact that we can trivialize the torsor
\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}\cong\operatorname{Alb}^{\Omega,(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
Recall now the following Proposition, which follows from
[
KSYR16
, Theorem 4.1.1]
(while the transfer structure follows from
[
SS03
, Proof of Lemma 3.2]
)

Proposition 3.22
.

The
k
-group scheme
\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
, regarded as étale sheaf on
\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, has a canonical structure of sheaf with transfers, and as such it has reciprocity in the sense of
[
KSYR16
, Definition 2.1.3]
.

Thanks to the Proposition, there is a unique map of preshaves with transfers

a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}

extending the map
a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon X\to\mathbf{Alb}^{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}}
defined above.

3.5.1.

When
\mathfrak{X}
is moreover smooth over
k
, we can apply Proposition
3.18
to get a map, defined over
k
,
{\rm alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(1)}\colon X\to\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
, universal for morphisms to torsors in
\mathcal{M}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. We can repeat the constructions of the previous point to get yet another
k
-group scheme
\mathbf{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
together with a canonical morphism

a_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}\colon X\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}.

The group
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
has by the same argument of Proposition
3.22
a canonical structure of étale sheaf with transfers, with reciprocity in the sense of
[
KSYR16
, Definition 2.1.3]
. This gives us a unique map of presheaves with transfers

a_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}

extending
a_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
. By Proposition
3.20
, we canonically identify
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
with
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\Omega}
.

3.6.
The maximal semi-abelian quotient

Let
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
be geometrically connected. Serre’s Albanese map
of Example
3.4
can be extended to a unique map of presheaves with transfers:

(3.22.1)

\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{X}

where
\mathbf{Alb}_{X}
is the semi-abelian Albanese scheme of
X
, defined by
[
Ram01
]
or
[
SS03
, Lemma 3.2]
using the same recipe of Section
3.5
. Since
\mathbf{Alb}_{X}
is semi-abelian, it is a homotopy invariant étale sheaf with transfers. Thus, taking sections over any field
L\supset k
, we have a factorization of the map (
3.22.1
) through

h_{0}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(X_{L})\to\mathbf{Alb}_{X}(L),

where
h_{0}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(X_{L})
denotes the zeroth Suslin homology group of
X_{L}=X\otimes_{k}L
. If
X
admits a
k
-point, the scheme
\mathbf{Alb}_{X}
decomposes as
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\times\operatorname{Alb}^{(0)}_{X}
, where
\operatorname{Alb}^{(0)}_{X}
denotes Serre’s semi-abelian Albanese variety of
X
. In particular, we get for any
L\supset k
algebraically closed an induced (surjective) map on the degree zero part

(3.22.2)

h_{0}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(X_{L})^{0}\to{\operatorname{Alb}}_{X_{L}}^{(0)}(L)=\operatorname{Alb}_{X_{L}}(L).

Now assume
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
.
Let
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
. By e.g.
[
BCKS17
, Proposition 2.6]
, there is a natural surjection
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X})\to h_{0}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(X)
, which can be composed with (
3.22.2
) to give a surjective homomorphism

{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{L})^{0}\to\operatorname{Alb}_{X_{L}}(L).

By Definition
3.15
, we have then that the object
({\rm alb}_{X},\operatorname{Alb}^{(1)}_{X},\operatorname{Alb}^{(0)}_{X})
belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
. By Proposition
3.18
, the universal property of
\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
gives a unique surjection

\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{(0)}_{X}

(and similarly for
\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(1)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
and
\operatorname{Alb}^{(1)}_{X}
), which factors through the semi-abelian quotient
\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathbf{SAb},\mathfrak{X}}
of
\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. It is straightforward to show that if
X
has a
k
-rational point, the algebraic groups
\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathbf{SAb},\mathfrak{X}}
and
\operatorname{Alb}_{X}^{(0)}
are isomorphic. We have therefore the following

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be as above, and suppose that
X
has a
k
-rational point. Then the semi-abelian part of
\operatorname{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}
agrees with Serre’s semi-abelian Albanese variety of
X
.

3.7.
Universal problem for presheaves with transfers

We suppose
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
and continue with the notations of
3.5
. As observed in Remark
2.9
(ii), for any
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
and for every section
g:\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to F
, there exists
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
such that g factors through
\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathfrak{X})
. In this case, we say that
g

has modulus

\mathfrak{X}
. We apply this to the case
\mathbf{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.

Proposition 3.24
.

Let
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
be geometrically connected and
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
.
Then the canonical map
a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
factors through
\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathfrak{X})
and it is universal with respect to this property: for any smooth commutative
k
-group scheme
G
, seen as étale reciprocity sheaf, and for any section
g\colon\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to G
with modulus
\mathfrak{X}
, there is a unique morphism
\tilde{g}\colon\mathbf{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to G
in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\mathfrak{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{g}}

Proof.

We first prove that
a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
factors through
\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathfrak{X})
. We have to show that for any smooth
k
-scheme
S
, the map
\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X)(S)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}(S)
factors through
h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})(S)
with
h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})=\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathfrak{X})
(cf. Remark
2.9
(ii)).
Since
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
, as any commutative
k
-group scheme, satisfies global injectivity, it is enough to check the factorization after passing to the function field
k(S)
of
S
, and in fact even to its algebraic closure. Let then
K\supset k
be an algebraically closed field, and look at the map
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)(K)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}_{K}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}(K)
. Let
\pi_{0}(X)
be the spectrum of the integral closure of
k
in
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
. The assignment
X\mapsto\pi_{0}(X)
is universal for morphisms from
X
into étale
k
-schemes. Since
X
is geometrically integral by assumption, we have
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\pi_{0}(X))=\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
(as étale sheaves), and the map
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X_{K})\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}_{K}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
induces then a map

\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X_{K})^{0}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}},(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}_{K}},

where
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X_{K})^{0}
denotes the kernel of
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X_{K})\to\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\pi_{0}(X_{K})).
We can then identify
\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X_{K})^{0}(K)
with the group of
0
-cycles of degree zero
Z_{0}(X_{K})^{0}
of
X_{K}
. Since
\mathfrak{X}
is a proper modulus pair, we have
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{K})^{0}=h_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{K})^{0}(K)
thanks to
[
KSY21
, Remark 2.2.3]
,
and thus the claim follows from Proposition
3.18
. The same argument proves the universal property as well.
∎

From now on, we write simply
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
for the
k
-group scheme
\mathbf{Alb}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. We end this section with the following result, which will be crucial for the construction of the category of
1
-reciprocity sheaves.

Lemma 3.25
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
3.24
, the map

a_{\mathfrak{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\colon\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is a surjective morphism of étale sheaves with transfers with rational coefficients.

Proof.

Let
{\rm Im}(a_{\mathfrak{X}})\subset\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
be the image of
a_{\mathfrak{X}}
in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
.
Since
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a smooth
k
-group scheme, we have
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by
[
KSY21
, Cor.3.2.5]
.
Let
C=\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}/{\rm Im}(a_{\mathfrak{X}})\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}
. We have to show that
a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}(C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=0
. By (
2.9.1
), we have that
a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}(C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, in particular it satisfies global injectivity by
[
Sai20
, Theorem 0.2]
, i.e. for any
Y\in\mathbf{Sm}
geometrically connected with function field
k(Y)
with algebraic closure
\overline{k(Y)}
there is an injective map

a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}(C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})(Y)\hookrightarrow a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}(C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})(k(Y))\hookrightarrow(C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})(\overline{k(Y)})=C(\overline{k(Y)})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}.

To complete the proof, it is then enough to show that
C(\overline{k(Y)})=0
. This follows from the fact that for any
K\supset k
algebraically closed, the map

\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X\otimes_{k}K)(K)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}(K)

is surjective, since
{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(\mathfrak{X}_{K})^{0}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{X}}(K)
is surjective.
∎

4.
The Albanese functors

4.1.
n
-reciprocity sheaves

For any
n\geq 0
, let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)_{\leq n}
be the category of finite correspondences on smooth
k
-schemes of dimension
\leq n
, and let
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}
be the category of modulus correspondences on smooth proper modulus pairs
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
with
\dim(\overline{X})\leq n
.
We let
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq n},\Lambda)
be the category of additive presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}
.

The natural inclusions of subcategory of objects of dimension
\leq n
give rise to a standard string of adjoint functors between the category of presheaves

(4.0.1)

(\sigma_{n,!},\sigma_{n}^{*}),\quad\sigma_{n,!}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq n},\Lambda)\leftrightarrows\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}{(k,\Lambda)}\colon\sigma_{n}^{*}.

Here, we follow the convention of
[
KMSY21a
]
for the left Kan extension of the restriction functor
\sigma_{n}^{*}
. Note that this is different from the one adopted in
[
ABV09
]
.

Remark 4.1
.

Let

(4.1.1)

(\sigma^{V}_{n,!},\sigma_{n}^{V,*}),\quad(\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\colon{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}(k_{\leq n},\Lambda)\leftrightarrows{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}{(k,\Lambda)}\colon\sigma_{n}^{V,*})

be the analogous adjoint functors from
[
ABV09
]
.
Since the functor
\omega
clearly restricts to a functor
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)_{\leq n}
, for all
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}(k,\Lambda)
we have that

\sigma_{n}^{*}\omega^{*}F\cong\omega^{*}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F\textrm{ in }\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq n},\Lambda).

By adjunction, we conclude that for all
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq n},\Lambda)
,

\sigma_{n,!}^{V}\omega_{!}F\cong\omega_{!}\sigma_{n,!}F\textrm{ in }{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}(k,\Lambda).

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)_{\leq n}
, for any modulus pair
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
, we have
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}
, hence
\sigma_{n}^{*}F(\mathfrak{X})=F(\mathfrak{X})

\omega_{!}\sigma_{n}^{*}F(X)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)}\sigma_{n}^{*}F(\mathfrak{X})=\omega_{!}F(X)=\sigma_{n}^{V,*}(\omega_{!}F)(X).

Finally, for every modulus pair
\mathfrak{X}
and every
\alpha\colon Y\to\omega(\mathfrak{X})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)
, by
[
KMSY21b
, Theorem 1.6.2]
there exists a proper modulus pair
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(Y)
and
\alpha^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)
such that
\alpha=\omega(\alpha^{\prime})
. In particular, since
\mathfrak{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}
, the system
\{F(Y)\}
for
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)_{\leq n}
running over the maps
Y\to\omega(X)
is cofinal in the system
\{F(\omega(\mathfrak{Y}))\}
for
\mathfrak{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}
running over the maps
\mathfrak{Y}\to\mathfrak{X}
. Hence we have that

\displaystyle\omega^{*}\sigma_{n,!}^{V}F(\mathfrak{X})=\sigma_{n,!}^{V}F(\omega(\mathfrak{X}))

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\omega(\mathfrak{X})\to Y)\\
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)_{\leq n}\end{subarray}}F(Y)

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})\\
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(k)_{\leq n}\end{subarray}}F(\omega(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y})\\
\mathfrak{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}\end{subarray}}F(\omega(\mathfrak{Y}))=\sigma_{n,!}\omega^{*}F(\mathfrak{X}).

Remark 4.2
.

The functors
\sigma_{n,!}
commute with colimits of presheaves since they are left adjoint. The functor
\sigma_{n}^{*}
also commutes with colimits of presheaves, since they are computed section-wise and
\sigma_{n}^{*}F(\mathfrak{X})=F(\mathfrak{X})
for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(k)_{\leq n}
.
In particular for all diagrams
\{F_{i}\}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
,
\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F_{i}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}F_{i}

The following lemma is mutuated from
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.16]
:

Lemma 4.3
.

The unit map
{\operatorname{Id}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma_{n}^{*}\sigma_{n,!}
is invertible.

Definition 4.4
.

We say that
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
is
n
-generated
(resp.
strongly
n
-generated
) if the counit map
\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}F\to F
is surjective (resp. an isomorphism).

Remark 4.5
.

If
F
is
n
-generated
(resp.
strongly
n
-generated
), then
\omega_{!}F
is
n
-generated (resp. strongly
n
-generated) in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
. Indeed, the functor
\omega_{!}
is exact and
\omega_{!}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}F=\sigma_{n,!}^{V}\sigma_{n}^{*,V}\omega_{!}F
by Remark
4.1
.

For example, if
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},X_{\infty})
with
\dim(\overline{X})\leq n
, then
\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathfrak{X})
is strongly
n
-generated.
The proof of the following Lemma is a diagram chase.

Lemma 4.6
.

Quotients and extensions of (strongly)
n
-generated sheaves are again (strongly)
n
-generated.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
. Following
[
ABV09
, Definition 1.1.20]
, we say that
F
is an
n
-modulus presheaf

if the natural map

h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}F)\to h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}F=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F

is an isomorphism of étale sheaves with transfers. Here, for any
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
, we denote by
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(G)
the étale sheaf with transfers
a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}G
, where
a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\colon{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)
is Voevodksy’s étale sheafification functor and
\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
is the composition
\omega_{!}\circ i^{{\overline{\square}}}
, where
i^{{\overline{\square}}}
is the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
, which has a right adjoint. Notice that the functor
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
is a composition of left adjoints, hence it commutes with all colimits.

We write
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
for the full subcategory of
n
-modulus presheaves.

The following is identical to
[
ABV09
, Remark 1.1.21]
:

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
be strongly
n
-generated. Then
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)
is an
n
-modulus sheaf.

Remark 4.9
.

Notice that in this case, differently from
[
ABV09
, Remark 1.1.21]
, if
F
is an
n
-modulus presheaf then it is not automatic that
F
is the
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}
of a strongly
n
-generated sheaf: we only know that the map

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}F\to F

is an isomorphism after applying
a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
.

Definition 4.10
.

We define the category of
n
-reciprocity sheaves

\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
as the essential image of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
via the functor
a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
.

The following result is immediate:

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)
. The following are equivalent

(i)

F
is an
n
-reciprocity sheaf;

(ii)

F\cong h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G
for some
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
.

Moreover, if the above conditions hold, we can take
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
in (ii).

Remark 4.12
.

If
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
, then
F
is an
n
-generated étale sheaf in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
. Indeed, for
F=h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G
with
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
, then there is a surjective map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)

a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}(F)=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{!}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G\twoheadrightarrow a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\square}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G\cong h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G=F.

Recall that the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
of
[
ABV09
, Definition 1.2.20]
, is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\Lambda)
of objects
F
such that

a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F\to a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}F=F
is an isomorphism.

Proposition 4.13
.

If
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
, then
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Take
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
.
By Remark
4.1
we have

(4.13.1)

h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}\omega^{*}F=h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\omega^{*}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F.

Notice that
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\omega^{*}=\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
, so by full faithfulness of
\omega^{*}
we conclude that

h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\omega^{*}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F=a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\omega^{*}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F=a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\omega_{!}\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F=a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\sigma^{V}_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}F=F.

In view of (
4.13.1
) and Lemma
4.11
, this implies
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq n}(k,\Lambda)
.
∎

The following lemma is analogue to
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.23]

Lemma 4.14
.

For any
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
, the natural map

(4.14.1)

\sigma_{n}^{V,*}h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G)\to\sigma_{n}^{V,*}a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G

induced by the counit map
\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G\to G
is an isomorphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k_{\leq n},\Lambda)

Corollary 4.15

(cfr.
[
ABV09
, Corollary 1.1.26]
)
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)
such that
F=\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G
with
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
and consider the natural map

h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G)=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G\to a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}G=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}F

induced by the counit map
\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G\to G
.
Let
N
be the kernel of the above map.
If
N
is an
n
-generated étale sheaf in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
, then it is zero.

Proof.

Since the functor
\sigma_{n}^{V,*}
is exact, we have by the definition of
N
an exact sequence of étale sheaves:

0\to\sigma_{n}^{V,*}(N)\to\sigma_{n}^{V,*}h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{n,!}\sigma_{n}^{*}G)\to\sigma_{n}^{V,*}a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}F,

hence by Lemma
4.14
we conclude
\sigma_{n}^{V,*}(N)=0
. Since
N
is
n
-generated, we have a surjective map of étale sheaves with transfers
\sigma_{n,!}^{V}\sigma_{n}^{V,*}(N)\to N
, showing that
N=0
. ∎

4.2.
0
-reciprocity sheaves

We specialize the general results of the previous section to the case
n=0
. By definition, the objects of the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}_{\leq 0}
of smooth modulus pairs of dimension
\leq 0
are the finite étale extensions
\ell\supset k
(with empty modulus divisor).
The essential image of the restriction of the functor
\omega\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
to
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}_{\leq 0}
induces an equivalence of categories

\omega_{|{\leq 0}}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}_{\leq 0}\simeq\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}_{\leq 0}

whose inverse is given by

\lambda_{|\leq 0}\colon\operatorname{Spec}(\ell)\mapsto(\operatorname{Spec}(\ell),\emptyset).

and induces an equivalence of categories

(4.15.1)

\omega_{|\leq 0,!}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda)\simeq{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda)\colon\lambda_{|\leq 0,!}.

Moreover, for any
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
with
X=\omega(\mathfrak{X})
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathfrak{X},(\operatorname{Spec}(\ell),\emptyset))\cong\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(\omega(\mathfrak{X}),\operatorname{Spec}(\ell))\cong\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(\pi_{0}(X),\operatorname{Spec}(\ell))\cong\mathbb{Z}^{\pi_{0}(|X\otimes_{k}\ell|)},

where
\pi_{0}(X)
is the spectrum of the integral closure of
k
in
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
and
\pi_{0}(|Y|)
for a scheme
Y
denotes the set of connected components of the underlying topological space
|Y|
. The first isomorphism follows from the fact that
\operatorname{Spec}(\ell)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}^{\rm prop}
(see
[
KMSY21a
, Lemma 1.5.1]
),
while the second and the third are classical (see
[
MVW06
, Lecture 1]
and
[
ABV09
, 1.2.1]
). From this we get an adjunction

(4.15.2)

\lambda_{|\leq 0}\circ\pi_{0}\circ\omega\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}\leftrightarrows\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}_{\leq 0}\colon\sigma_{0}.

We let
\Pi_{0}
denote
\lambda_{|\leq 0}\circ\pi_{0}\circ\omega
.
Passing to the categories of presheaves, we have that the functor
\sigma_{0,!}
of (
4.0.1
) has a left adjoint:

(4.15.3)

\Pi_{0,!}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)\leftrightarrows\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda)\colon\Pi_{0}^{*}=\sigma_{0,!}.

In particular,
\sigma_{0,!}
is given explicitly by

(4.15.4)

\sigma_{0,!}(F)(\mathfrak{X})=F(\Pi_{0}(\mathfrak{X}))=F(\pi_{0}(\omega(\mathfrak{X})),\emptyset)\;\text{ for }F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda).

The following Corollary shows that the category of
0
-reciprocity sheaves is simply equivalent to the category of
0
-motivic sheaves in the sense of Ayoub–Barbieri-Viale.

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
, then
\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
. In particular, every
0
-reciprocity sheaf is strongly
0
-generated in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
, and we have equivalences

(4.16.1)

\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 0}(k,\Lambda)\simeq\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 0}(k,\Lambda)\simeq\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda).

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
and
X=\omega(\mathfrak{X})
. By(
4.15.4
), we have that

\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G(\mathfrak{X}\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1})=\sigma_{0}^{*}G(\pi_{0}(\omega(\mathfrak{X}\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1})),\emptyset).

On the other hand, we have that
\omega(\mathfrak{X}\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1})=X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}
and as observed in the proof of
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.2.2]
, we have that
\pi_{0}(X\times\mathbf{A}^{1})=\pi_{0}(X)
, hence

\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G(\mathfrak{X}\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1})=\sigma_{0}^{*}G(\pi_{0}(\omega(\mathfrak{X}\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1})),\emptyset)=\sigma_{0}^{*}G(\pi_{0}(\omega(\mathfrak{X})),\emptyset)=\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G(\mathfrak{X}).

Hence
\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
proving the first assertion.
Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 0}(k,\Lambda)
and let
F=h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G^{\prime}
with

G^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
be as in Lemma
4.11
(ii).
Then
\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\Lambda)
,
so

F=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G^{\prime}\simeq a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{!}\sigma_{0,!}\sigma_{0}^{*}G^{\prime}=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\sigma_{0,!}^{V}\sigma_{0}^{V,*}\omega_{!}G^{\prime}.

This implies that
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda)
. On the other hand, by Proposition
4.13
we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 0}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 0}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k_{\leq 0},\Lambda).}

By
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.2.2]
, the composition above is an equivalence, hence we deduce the equivalences of (
4.16.1
).
∎

4.3.
1
-reciprocity sheaves and Albanese functors

In this subsection, we will assume that
k
has characteristic zero. In particular,
k
satisfies resolutions of singularities and for any smooth proper modulus pair, we can identify
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\operatorname{CH}}}
with
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\Omega}
, and we simply write
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
for the Albanese scheme of
\mathfrak{X}
.

Remark 4.17
.

If
\mathfrak{C}=(\overline{C},C_{\infty})
denotes a
1
-dimensional smooth and proper modulus pair with
\overline{C}
geometrically integral, by Lemma
4.8
,
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})
is a
1
-reciprocity sheaf. Moreover, as observed in (
2.9.2
), we have:

h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathfrak{C}))=a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}h_{0}(\mathfrak{C})=\underline{\operatorname{Pic}}(\overline{C},C_{\infty}),

where
\underline{\operatorname{Pic}}(\overline{C},C_{\infty})
denotes the relative Picard group scheme, whose connected component of the identity
\underline{\operatorname{Pic}}^{0}(\overline{C},C_{\infty})
agrees with the Rosenlicht-Serre generalized Jacobian
{\rm Jac}(\overline{C},C_{\infty})
. By Proposition
3.24
and the proof of Proposition
3.18
, we have
\underline{\operatorname{Pic}}(\overline{C},C_{\infty})={{\bf Alb}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
. Hence we finally have that
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})={{\bf Alb}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is represented by a commutative group scheme.

Remark 4.18
.

More generally, let
\mathfrak{X}
be a smooth and proper modulus pair. Then the sheaf
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is
1
-generated. In fact,
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
can be written as extension of a semi-abelian
k
-group scheme (i.e. a
k
-group scheme
G
such that
G^{0}
is a semi-abelian variety) and a unipotent algebraic group
U
. In characteristic
0
, the group
U
is a product of
\mathbf{G}_{a}
, and
\mathbf{G}_{a}
is a direct summand of
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},2\infty)
, so that it is
1
-generated. The semi-abelian
k
-group scheme
G
is a quotient of the generalized Jacobian of a suitable curve contained in
G
by a theorem of Matsusaka
[
Mat52
]
, which is
1
-generated by the previous remark (see
[
ABV09
, 1.3]
). Hence
G
itself is
1
-generated by
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.15]
. Applying again
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.15]
to
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
we get the statement.

We deduce from the previous remarks the following analogue to
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.3.4]
.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\Lambda)
. Then any subsheaf of
F
is a
1
-generated étale sheaf in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
.

Proof.

We essentially follow the steps in the proof of
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.3.4]
, starting from the case of
F=h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})
, for
\mathfrak{C}
a smooth and proper modulus pair of dimension
1
. This is a
1
-reciprocity sheaf by Remark
4.17
. Let
E\subset F
be a subsheaf.
Since colimits of
1
-generated étale sheaves are
1
-generated étale sheaves,
(see
4.2
), we can assume that
E
is the image of a map
a\colon\Lambda_{tr}(X)\to h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})
, for
X\in\mathbf{Sm}
. Since for
k\subseteq k^{\prime}
a finite extension, the map
X_{k^{\prime}}\to X
is an étale cover: this implies that
\Lambda_{tr}(X_{k^{\prime}})\to\Lambda_{tr}(X)
is a surjective map of étale sheaves, so we can assume
X
geom. connected. By Remark
4.17
, we have then a map

a\colon\Lambda_{tr}(X)\to{\bf Alb}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cong h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C}).

Since
{\bf Alb}_{\mathfrak{C}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\Lambda)
, by Remark
2.9
there exists a smooth proper modulus pair
\mathfrak{X}
with
X=\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}
such that
a
factors through
h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})
, and by Proposition
3.24
, it uniquely factors through
a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
:

\textstyle{h_{0}(\mathfrak{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathfrak{X}}}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\bf Alb}_{\mathfrak{C}}.}

By Lemma
3.25
, the motivic Albanese map
a_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a surjective morphism of étale sheaves, hence the image of
a
agrees with the image of
a^{\prime}
. Thus
E={\rm Im}(a^{\prime})
is a
1
-generated étale sheaf by
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.15]
and Remark
4.18
.

Now the general case. Let
E\subset F\cong h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G)
with
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k,\Lambda)
(cf. Lemma
4.11
(ii)).
The sheaf
\sigma_{1}^{*}G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq 1},\Lambda)
can be written as colimit of representable sheaves in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}(k_{\leq 1},\Lambda)
, i.e.

\sigma_{1}^{*}G=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathfrak{C}\to\sigma_{1}^{*}G}\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathfrak{C})_{\leq 1},\quad\mathfrak{C}=(\overline{C},C_{\infty}),\quad\dim(\overline{C})=1.

Since
\sigma_{1,!}
and
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
commute with colimits (being left adjoints), we have then

(4.19.1)

F\cong h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}(\sigma_{1}^{*}G))=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathfrak{C})_{\leq 1})=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C}).

For any
\mathfrak{C}\in(\mathfrak{C}\to\sigma_{1}^{*}G)_{\leq 1}
, we have in particular a map
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})\to F
, and thus a map from the fiber product

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathfrak{C}\to\sigma_{1,*}F}(h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})\times_{F}E)\to E

which is surjective (the proof of surjectivity is formal and identical to the corresponding statement in the proof of
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.3.4]
). Now it is enough to notice that each
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})\times_{F}E\subset h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{C})
is a
1
-generated étale sheaf by the previous step and the fact that
1
-generated étale sheaves are stable by colimits. To conclude we apply again
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.15]
.
∎

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
be an étale sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces which is
1
-generated in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
. If it is a reciprocity sheaf, then it is a
1
-reciprocity sheaf. In particular, any subsheaf of a
1
-reciprocity sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces is again a
1
-reciprocity sheaf and the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is closed under taking subobjects, colimits and extensions in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.19
, any subsheaf of a
1
-reciprocity sheaf is again
1
-generated, and by
[
KSY21
, Corollary 2.4.2]
any subsheaf of a reciprocity sheaf is a reciprocity sheaf. Then the second part of the Proposition follows from the first, since
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is an abelian category stable by colimits in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
(here we are using the fact that we consider
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients in order to exploit Proposition
2.1
, since
[
KSY21
, Corollary 2.4.2]
is a statement about the Nisnevich sheafification) and
1
-generated étale sheaves are stable by colimits and extensions by
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.1.15]
.

We now prove the first assertion. Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
be a
1
-generated étale sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces and suppose that
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, i.e. that there exists
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
such that
F=\omega_{!}G
. By Remark
4.1
, we have that

\sigma^{V}_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{V,*}F=\omega_{!}\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G

and the counit
\sigma^{V}_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{V,*}F\to F
is the image via
\omega_{!}
of the counit
\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G\to G
. Since
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, the map
\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G\to G
factors through
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G
, which induces a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\omega_{!}\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\sigma^{V}_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{V,*}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*)}

Since
F
is a
1
-generated étale sheaf, the map
(*)
is surjective, hence the induced map
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G)\to F
is surjective.
Let
N=\mathrm{ker}(h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G)\to F)
.
By Lemma
4.11
,
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
so that
N
is a
1
-generated étale sheaf by Lemma
4.19
. Hence
N=0
by Corollary
4.15
so that
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\sigma_{1,!}\sigma_{1}^{*}G)\cong F
, which concludes the proof by Lemma
4.11
.
∎

Recall that
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is a Grothendieck abelian category by
[
KSY21
, Corollary 2.4.2]
. The following corollary is immediate from the previous proposition.

Corollary 4.21
.

The inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
(and consequently

\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
) is exact, and the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is a Grothendieck abelian category (in particular, it has enough injectives).

The proof of the following Lemma is identical to the proof of
[
ABV09
, Lemma 1.3.6]
, using Lemma
4.19
.

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
be a smooth commutative
k
-group scheme and let
F
be an étale subsheaf of
G
with transfers such that its sheaf of connected components
\pi_{0}(F)
is zero. Then
F
is represented by a closed subgroup of
G
.

Definition 4.23
.

A
1
-reciprocity sheaf
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is called
finitely generated
if there exists a commutative
k
-group scheme
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
with
\pi_{0}(G)
finitely generated together with a surjection
q\colon G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to F
.
If the kernel of
q
is itself finitely generated, we say that
F
is
finitely presented
.

We write
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
for the full subcategory of finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaves. An almost word-by-word translation of
[
ABV09
, Proposition 1.3.8]
, using Lemma
4.19
and Lemma
4.22
gives the following canonical presentation of every finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaf. This result will be repeatedly used in the rest of the paper.

Proposition 4.24
.

Any
1
-reciprocity sheaf is filtered colimit of finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaves. If
F
is a finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaf, then there is a unique and functorial exact sequence

0\to L\to G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to F\to 0

where
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
is a smooth commutative
k
-group scheme such that
\pi_{0}(G)
is finitely generated and
L\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 0}(k,\mathbb{Q})\cong\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k_{\leq 0},\mathbb{Q})
is a finitely generated
0
-motivic sheaf.

Remark 4.25
.

For a smooth commutative
k
-group scheme
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
, write
G^{sab}
for its semi-abelian quotient: it is a smooth commutative
k
-group scheme such that the connected component of the identity
(G^{sab})^{0}
is a semi-abelian variety. We have by Chevalley’s theorem an extension

(4.25.1)

0\to U\to G\to G^{sab}\to 0

where
U
is a unipotent group. Since
k
is of characteristic zero,
U\cong\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r}
where
r
is the
unipotent rank
of
G
.
More generally, for any
F
finitely presented
1
-motivic sheaf, by Proposition
4.24
we have a functorial commutative diagram

(4.25.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{sab}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

where
F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}:=h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
, and since
\mathrm{ker}(G\to G^{sab})
is unipotent, by
[
MVW06
, Example 2.23]
we also conclude that
C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)\simeq F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}[0]
, where
C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
is the Suslin complex. Moreover, by the right exactness of
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
, the kernel of the map
G^{sab}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
is a quotient of the lattice
L
, hence it is itself a lattice.

In general, if
F=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F_{1}
is a
1
-reciprocity sheaf with
F_{i}
finitely presented, then
F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}(F_{i}^{{A^{1}}})
and
\mathrm{ker}(F\twoheadrightarrow F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\mathrm{ker}(F_{i}\twoheadrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})
. By (
4.25.2
), for each
i
there is a lattice
L^{\prime}_{i}
and a finite dimensional
k
-vector space
U_{i}
such that

\mathrm{ker}(F_{i}\twoheadrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\cong(U_{i}\otimes\mathbf{G}_{a})/L^{\prime}_{i}.

Since
\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}(U_{i}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong(\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}U_{i})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}
, which is (non canonically) isomorphic to a (possibly infinite) sum of
\mathbf{G}_{a}
, and
\mathcal{L}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}L_{i}
is a
0
-motivic sheaf, we have an exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{L}\to\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a}\to\mathrm{ker}(F_{i}\twoheadrightarrow F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\to 0.

Thus, for every
1
-reciprocity sheaf
F
, we have an exact sequence

(4.25.3)

0\to(\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})/\mathcal{L}\to F\to F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\to 0

describing
F
as an extension of an
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaf by a quotient of a (possibly infinite dimensional) vector group by a
0
-motivic sheaf.

Recall that by
[
Isa01
, Prop. 11.1]
, if
\mathcal{C}
has all colimits, then
\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}
has all colimits. We can now prove the following generalization to
[
ABV09
, Proposition 1.3.11]
.

Theorem 4.26
.

Let
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
.
The embedding
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
has a pro-left adjoint:

\operatorname{Alb}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})

induced by colimit from

\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\mapsto``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
smooth.

Proof.

For
X\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
, recall from Definition
2.8
the cofiltered category of Cartier compactifications
\mathbf{Comp}(X)
.
It is enough to show that for any
X
, for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
with total space smooth (since
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, such choice exists), and for any
E\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, we have

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k)}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},E)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k)}(\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X),E).

By Galois descent it is also enough to prove the claim for
k
algebraically closed. By Lemma
3.25
, for any
\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}
the Albanese map
a_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X)\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is a surjective morphism of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, hence since filtered colimits are exact, we only need to show that

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k)}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},E)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k)}(\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X),E)

is surjective, i.e. that for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
, every map
\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to E
factors through
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
for some
n
.
Since
\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)
is compact in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, by Proposition
4.24
we can suppose that
E
is finitely presented. Let
E=\mathrm{coker}(L\hookrightarrow G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})
be as in Proposition
4.24
.
Then we get a long exact sequence

\ldots\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X),G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X),E)\to H^{1}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X,L)\to\ldots.

Since
L\cong\mathbb{Q}^{r}
, being
k
separably closed,

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X,L)=H^{1}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(X,\mathbb{Q}^{r})=0
. Thus the map

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X),G)\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X),E)

is surjective, i.e. every map
s\colon\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X)\to E
factors via
\sigma\colon\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
. By Remark
2.9
(iii) for every
\sigma
as above and for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
, there exists
n
such that
s
factors through
h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)})
. Considering now
G
with integral coefficients, we deduce that there exists
m
such that
m\sigma
defines a section of
G
over
X
with modulus
\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}
as in
3.7
, hence by Proposition
3.24
,
m\sigma
factors through
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}
, so
\sigma
factors through
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, concluding the proof.
∎

4.4.
The log Albanese functor

We now generalize this to the logarithmic setting. As in Definition
2.8
, we introduce the following

Definition 4.27
.

Let
X=({\underline{X}},\partial X)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and
|\partial X|_{{\operatorname{red}}}
be the reduced divisor on
{\underline{X}}
associated to
\partial X
. We let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
be the cofiltered category formed by the modulus pairs
(\overline{X},\widetilde{|\partial X|}_{{\operatorname{red}}}+D)
, where
(\overline{X},D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}({\underline{X}})
and
\widetilde{|\partial X|}_{{\operatorname{red}}}\subset\overline{X}
is an effective Cartier divisor supported on the closure of
|\partial X|_{{\operatorname{red}}}
in
\overline{X}
such that
\widetilde{|\partial X|}_{{\operatorname{red}}}\times_{\overline{X}}{\underline{X}}=|\partial X|_{{\operatorname{red}}}
.

Let

\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\colon\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\textrm{d\'{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})

be the composition in (
2.10.1
), which is fully faithful and exact.
As observed in Remark
2.11
,
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
is fully faithful, exact and commutes with all colimits.

Theorem 4.28
.

Let
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
. The fully faithful exact functor

\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}}\mathbf{Shv}_{\mathrm{d}{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})

has a pro-left adjoint, called the log Albanese functor

\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{\mathrm{d}{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\textrm{pro-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}).

induced by colimit from

\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\mapsto``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

for any choice of
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
smooth.

Proof.

We proceed as in the non-log case. Since
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}
commutes with filtered colimits, we can reduce to prove the adjunction for maps against
E
finitely presented, quotient of a smooth commutative group
k
-scheme
G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
by a lattice
L
. As before, it is enough to prove the claim with
k
algebraically closed, by Galois descent. Since we are considering sheaves of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces, we can assume that
L\cong\mathbb{Q}^{r}
. For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
we have

H^{1}_{\textrm{d\'{e}t}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(L))\cong H^{1}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X-|\partial X|,\mathbb{Q}^{r})=0.

So following the steps of the previous proof, it is enough to show that for
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
, any map

\sigma:\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

factors through
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})
for some
n
, and conclude by full faithfulness of
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
.
On the other hand,
for
X=({\underline{X}},\partial X)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
let
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},\widetilde{|\partial X|}_{{\operatorname{red}}}+D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
as in Definition
4.27
, and for
n\geq 1
let
\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}=(\overline{X},\widetilde{|\partial X|}_{{\operatorname{red}}}+nD)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
. We have
(see §
2.3
for the notation)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\mathbf{G}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})(X)

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}),G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}),G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{logCI}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)})),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})),

where the first equality follows from Proposition
2.2
,
the second follows from the definition of
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}
in
[
Sai21
]
and Remark
2.9
, the third from the isomorphsim

(a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong a_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{V}\omega_{!}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{X})\quad\textrm{(see Def. \ref{def;hetrec})}

and the fourth from the full faithfulness of
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
.
So, there exists
n
such that
\sigma
factors through
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}h_{0,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{(n)})
, hence again there exists
m
such that
m\sigma
is a section of
G
with modulus
\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}
as in
3.7
, so by Proposition
3.24
and full faithfulness of
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
, it factors through
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})
, proving the claim.
∎

5.
Extension of 1-reciprocity sheaves

In this section, we prove some technical results about extensions in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. First of all, recall the Breen–Deligne resolution, whose proof is contained in
[
CS
, Appendix to Lecture IV]
:

Theorem 5.1

(Eilenberg–Maclane, Breen, Deligne, Clausen–Scholze)
.

Let
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
. Then there exists a complex of presheaves (the Breen–Deligne resolution)
C_{\bullet}(G)
such that
C_{i}(G)=\mathbb{Z}(G^{\times t_{i}})
for some integer
t_{i}>0
together with an augmentation map
s\colon C(G)\to G
in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Cpx}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
that is an equivalence in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
.
Moreover, the first terms are computed as follows:

\mathbb{Z}[G^{\times 3}\times G^{\times 2}]\xrightarrow{\partial_{2}}\mathbb{Z}[G\times G]\xrightarrow{\partial_{1}}\mathbb{Z}[G]\xrightarrow{\partial_{0}}G\to 0

•

\partial_{0}
is the sum map,

•

\partial_{1}([x,y])=[x+y]-[x]-[y]

•

\partial_{2}([x,y,z,t,u])=[x+y,z]-[x,y+z]-[y,z]+[x,y]+[t,u]-[u,t]
.

Remark 5.2
.

Every
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
is a compact object in
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
: since
G
is an extension of
G^{0}
and
\pi_{0}(G)
, it is enough to show that
G^{0}
and
\pi_{0}(G)
are compact. By our assumption,
\pi_{0}(G)
is finitely generated, so it is compact. Since
G^{0}\in\mathbf{Sm}(k)
by assumption, it is is compact in
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\mathbf{Sm}(k),\mathrm{Sets})
, and since the forgetful functor
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\mathbf{Sm}(k),\mathbb{Z})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\mathbf{Sm}(k),\mathrm{Sets})
preserves filtered colimits,
G^{0}
is compact in
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\mathbf{Sm}(k),\mathbb{Z})
too.
Moreover, since the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
preserves filtered colimits, we have that
G
is a compact object in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
too.

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
. Then

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\rm fppf}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G_{1},G_{2})\cong\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G_{1},G_{2})

Proof.

By the Breen–Deligne resolution, for
\tau\in\{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},{\rm fppf}\}
we have

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\tau}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G_{1},G_{2})\simeq R\Gamma_{\tau}(G_{1}^{\times t_{\bullet}},G_{2})

and by a theorem of Grothendieck (see
[
Mil80
, III, Theorem 3.9]
):

R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(G_{1}^{\times t_{\bullet}},G_{2})\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm fppf}(G_{1}^{\times t_{\bullet}},G_{2}),

which allows us to conclude.
∎

Recall the following result proved in
[
AHPL16
, Appendix B]
:

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
and let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. Let
i^{\rm tr}
be the functor “forget transfers”. Then

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},i^{\rm tr}F)\cong\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},F)

In particular,
G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is a compact object in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

The second part follows from the first and Remark
5.2
since
i^{{\operatorname{tr}}}
preserves filtered colimits. Recall the left adjoint
L\gamma^{*}
of
i^{\rm tr}
, which is computed by colimits by the formula

L\gamma^{*}(\mathbb{Q}(X)[i]):=(\gamma^{*}\mathbb{Q}(X))[i]=\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)[i].

If
C_{\bullet}(G)\to G
is the Breen–Deligne resolution, we have that

L\gamma^{*}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=L\gamma^{*}(C_{\bullet}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=\gamma^{*}(C_{\bullet}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}),

and the last equality follows from the fact that
C_{i}(G)
is representable. This gives the following equivalence:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},i^{\rm tr}F)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(L\gamma^{*}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}),F)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(\gamma^{*}(C_{\bullet}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}),F)

By
[
AHPL16
, Lemma B.4]
, we have an equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
:

\gamma^{*}(C_{\bullet}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\simeq G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

which allows us to conclude.
∎

We need the following easy but fundamental result:

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
G\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
. Then for all
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
we have an equivalence:

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},F\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

Proof.

By adjunction we have

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},F\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}).

Notice that the tensor product is not derived since
\mathbb{Q}
is flat. Consider the fiber sequence in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
:

F\to F\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{m}F\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}

Then since the colimit is filtered and
G
is compact, we have a fiber sequence

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\to\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}).

On the other hand, since
\mathbb{Q}
is flat,
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
commutes with homotopy groups, so since
\pi_{i}
and
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
commute with filtered colimits, we have that

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\bigl((\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\bigr)

\displaystyle\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\bigl(\pi_{i}(\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\bigr).

Since
\pi_{i}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(G,F\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})
are
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}
-modules for all
i
, the above formula is equal to zero, so we conclude.
∎

Finally, we can deduce the following result, which (for the most part) is a well-known application of the Breen–Deligne resolution:

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
. Then the groups
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(G_{1},G_{2})
are torsion for
n\geq 2

Proof.

If
G_{2}
is discrete, this exactly
[
BVK16
, Proposition 3.2.1, (i)]
. If
G_{2}
is connected, as pointed out in
[
BVK16
, Proposition 3.2.1, (ii)]
this can be deduced from Breen’s method from
[
Bre69
]
. In fact, let
X
be the underlying smooth scheme of
G_{1}
: using the Breen–Deligne resolution of Theorem
5.1
, one reduces to show that
H^{q}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X^{\times s},G_{2})
is torsion for
q\geq 2
. If
G_{2}
is represented by a semi-abelian variety, this was pointed out in
[
Bre69
, §7,8, and 9]
. If
G_{2}=\mathbf{G}_{a}
, then
