H^{q}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X^{\times s},\mathbf{G}_{a})=H^{q}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(X^{\times s},\mathbf{G}_{a})
:

(i)

If
G_{1}
is discrete, then
\dim(X^{\times s})=0
so
H^{q}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(X^{\times s},\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
for
q\geq 1
.

(ii)

If
G_{1}=\mathbf{G}_{m}
or
\mathbf{G}_{a}
, then
X^{\times s}
is affine so
H^{q}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(X^{\times s},\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
for
q\geq 1
.

By Chevalley’s classification, we are left to the case where
G_{1}
is an abelian variety. In this case, since
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, we have that
\mathbf{G}_{a}
is already a sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces, so by Corollary
5.4
and Proposition
2.2
we have:

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(G_{1}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(G_{1},\mathbf{G}_{a}).

So we will conclude by the following:

Claim 5.7
.

Let
G_{1}
be an abelian variety. Then
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(G_{1},\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
for
n\geq 2

Proof.

For a curve
C\subseteq G_{1}
intersection of ample divisors, the map
J_{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to G_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is split surjective, which implies that we have a split inclusion:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(G_{1},\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(G_{1}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\mathbf{G}_{a})\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(J_{C}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(J_{C},\mathbf{G}_{a}),

which reduces to the case where
G_{1}=J_{C}
.
Since
J_{C}
is an abelian variety, it is a birational sheaf in the sense of
[
KS17
]
, so for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
we have an isomorphism

J_{C}({\underline{X}})\cong J_{C}(X^{\circ})

which implies that we have an isomorphism of
{\operatorname{dNis}}
sheaves (cf. Remark
2.13
) :

\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}(J_{C})\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}(J_{C}).

Since
\mathbf{G}_{a}\cong\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\mathbf{G}_{a})
, the adjunction
(
2.13.2
) implies an equivalence

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(J_{C},\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(\omega^{*}_{\log}J_{C},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}\mathbf{G}_{a}).

On the other hand, since
J_{C}[0]
is a direct summand of the Suslin–Voevodsky complex
C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(C)
in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\rm tr}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
by
[
Voe00
, Section 3.4]
, we have that
\omega^{*}_{\log}J_{C}[0]
is a direct summand of
\iota(M(C,\mathrm{triv}))
by
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 8.2.11]
, where
\iota
is the inclusion of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
. Since now
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\mathbf{G}_{a})
is
{\overline{\square}}
-local, we have an injective map:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(\omega^{*}_{\log}J_{C},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}\mathbf{G}_{a})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\pi_{-n}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\rm ltr}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(M(C,\mathrm{triv}),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\mathbf{G}_{a}))

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{-n}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(M(C,\mathrm{triv}),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\mathbf{G}_{a}))

\displaystyle\cong H^{n}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})

and the latter is zero for
n\geq 2
. This allows us to conclude.

∎

Remark 5.8
.

Recall that if
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\star}
, then there exist
L\to G

as in Proposition
4.24
such that

0\to L\to G\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to F\to 0.

By Remark
5.2
and
5.4
(plus the fact that
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is closed under colimits in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
),
L
and
G
are compact in both
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, which implies that
F
is compact in both
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

We are now ready to prove the following:

Proposition 5.9
.

The category
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is closed under extensions in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

Let
F_{1},F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. Since
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is a full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
by Proposition
2.10
, we have that an extension in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
splits in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
if and only if it splits in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, so

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2}).

By Proposition
4.24
, we can write
F_{1}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F_{i_{1}}
with
F_{i_{1}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
. In particular, there is a surjective map
\oplus F_{i_{1}}\twoheadrightarrow F_{1}.

Let
K
be its kernel: we have the following commutative diagram with exact rows:

(5.9.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(K,F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(F_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(F_{i_{1}},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(K,F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(K,F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(F_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(F_{i_{1}},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(K,F_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(**)}

so by the five-lemma
(*)
is surjective if
(**)
is surjective, hence we can suppose
F_{1}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. Since
F_{1}
is compact in both
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
by Remark
5.8
and filtered colimits are exact, again by
4.24
it is enough to suppose that
F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.
Let
F_{i}=\mathrm{coker}(L_{i}\hookrightarrow G_{i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})
as in Proposition
4.24
, we have a commutative square with exact rows (we omit the
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
here):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(G_{1},G_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(G_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(G_{1},L_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(G_{1},G_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(G_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(G_{1},L_{2}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(3)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(2)}

By Proposition
5.5
and Corollary
5.3
, we have that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(G_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q},G_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\rm fppf}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(G_{1},G_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}.

By
[
Mil17
, Exercise 5-10]
we have that
\mathcal{G}^{*}
is closed by extensions in
fppf
sheaves, which implies that if
E\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(G_{1},G_{2})
, there exists
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{*}
such that
E=G^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, in particular
E
is in the image of
(1)
. This implies that
(1)
is an isomorphism.
Moreover,
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(G_{1},L_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}=0
by Proposition
5.6
, which implies that
(2)
is surjective, so
(3)
is surjective, hence an isomorphism.
We have now the following commutative diagram with exact rows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(F_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(G_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(L_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(F_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(G_{1},F_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(L_{1},F_{2}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

and we conclude using the 5-lemma.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

For all
F_{1},F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0\quad\textrm{for }i\geq 2.

Proof.

First suppose that
F_{1}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
, hence it is a compact object in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. Since filtered colimits are exact, as in the proof of Proposition
5.9
we can assume that
F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. For
i=1,2
, let
F_{i}=\mathrm{coker}(L_{i}\hookrightarrow G_{i})
be as in Proposition
4.24
(again, we omit
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
). Since
L_{1}
is a lattice, we have that
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(L_{1},F_{2})=0
for
i\geq 1
, so we can suppose
F_{1}=G_{1}
. Since by Proposition
5.6
, we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(G_{1},L_{2})=\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(G_{1},G_{2})=0\quad\textrm{for }i\geq 2,

we get
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0
for
i\geq 2
, which concludes
the case
F_{1}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
.
In general, let
0\to\bigoplus\mathbf{G}_{a}/\mathcal{L}_{1}\to F_{1}\to F_{1}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\to 0
be as in (
4.25.3
).
Then we have a long exact sequence sequence

(5.10.1)

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}},F_{2})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a}/\mathcal{L}_{1},F_{2}).

As in
[
ABV09
, Proposition 2.4.10]
,
\mathcal{L}_{1}
splits as a direct sum
\mathcal{L}_{1}\cong\oplus_{a}\mathcal{L}_{a_{1}}
of finitely presented
0
-motivic sheaves, which implies that

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{L}_{1},F_{2})\cong\prod\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{L}_{a_{1}},F_{2})=0\quad\textrm{for }i\geq 1.

Moreover,
\mathbf{G}_{a}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
, so by the previous case:

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a},F_{2})\cong\prod\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathbf{G}_{a},F_{2})=0\quad\textrm{for }i\geq 2.

This imples that the last term of (
5.10.1
) vanishes for
i\geq 2
. In order to conclude, it is enough to show that
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}},F_{2})=0
. In other words, we can suppose that
F_{1}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
. Arguing as before, consider now the exact sequence

0\to\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a}/\mathcal{L}_{2}\to F_{2}\to F_{2}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\to 0.

It induces a long exact sequence

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},\bigoplus\mathbf{G}_{a}/\mathcal{L}_{2})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})

By
[
ABV09
, Proposition 2.4.10]
we have that for
i\geq 2

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})=\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})=0,

so we need to show that

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})=0\;\text{ for $i\geq 2$. }

As in the proof of
[
ABV09
, Proposition 2.4.10]
, we can reduce to separately analyze the following cases:

(i)

F_{1}=\mathcal{L}
is a
0
-motivic sheaf.

(ii)

F_{1}=\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathbf{G}_{m}
, where
\mathcal{L}
is a
0
-motivic sheaf.

(iii)

F_{1}\simeq\bigoplus_{\beta}A_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, where
A_{\beta}
are abelian varieties.

If
\mathcal{L}
is
0
-motivic, again it splits as a direct sum
\bigoplus\mathcal{L}_{a}
of finitely presented
0
-motivic sheaves.
In particular

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{L}\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\prod\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{L}_{a},\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a}),

and

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathbf{G}_{m},\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\prod\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{L}_{a}\otimes\mathbf{G}_{m},\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})

Since now both
\mathcal{L}_{a}
and
\mathcal{L}_{a}\otimes\mathbf{G}_{m}
are in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
, so they are compact objects in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, we can put the direct sum outside the Ext and conclude the vanishing for
i\geq 2
in case (i) and (ii) thanks to Proposition
5.6
.
In case (iii),
A_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
, so

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\oplus_{\beta}A_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})

\displaystyle=\prod_{\beta}\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(A_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\oplus\mathbf{G}_{a})

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{\beta}\bigoplus\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(A_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\mathbf{G}_{a})

and the last term vanishes by Claim
5.7
.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

For
F_{1},F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})\;\text{ for all $i\geq 0$.}

In particular the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
has cohomological dimension
1
, i.e. for
i\geq 2
we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0.

Proof.

The case
i=0
is Proposition
2.10
and
i=1
is Proposition
5.9
. In light of Lemma
5.10
, the first part for
i\geq 2
follows from the second. Let
0\to F_{2}\to I\to B\to 0
with
I
injective in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
,
so that for
i\geq 2
we have that

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{i-1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},B).

Notice that by Proposition
5.9
, we have a commutative diagram where the vertical maps are isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},B).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*2)}

Since
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0
by Lemma
5.10
, the map
(*1)
is surjective, so the map
(*2)
is surjective. Since
I
is injective,
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},I)=0
, so
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},B)=0
. We conclude that:

\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0.

which concludes the case
i=2
. In general, suppose that
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0
for all
F_{1},F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, in particular
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},B)=0
, so we conclude that
\operatorname{Ext}^{i+1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(F_{1},F_{2})=0
, which by induction finishes the proof.
∎

Theorem 5.12
.

Every complex in
C\in\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is formal, i.e. we have an equivalence:

C\simeq\bigoplus\pi_{i}(C)[i]

Proof.

By Proposition
5.11
, we have that for every
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
, and
i\geq 2
,

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\pi_{j}C,F)=0,

so the spectral sequence

(5.12.1)

E_{2}^{ij}=\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\pi_{j}C,F)\Rightarrow\pi_{i+j}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(C,F[0])

degenerates at page 2, which allows us to conclude by
[
Del68
, Prop (1.2) and Rem (1.4)]

∎

6.
The derived Albanese functor

Assume that
k
has characteristic zero.
The goal of this section is to show the existence of a left derived Albanese functor in the sense of Definition
A.10
. To ease the notation, we will write
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
, resp.
\mathbf{logCI}
) for
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, resp.
\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}
) and we will identify it with
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{\textrm{d\'{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, resp.
\mathbf{logCI}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
) by Proposition
2.1
(resp. Proposition
2.2
). We also write
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
for

\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.
In this section we let
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
denote the functor from Theorem
4.28
:

(6.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}.}

By Remark
2.11
and Proposition
4.20
this functor is fully faithful, exact and commutes with all colimits.

We announce the main theorem (cfr.
[
ABV09
, Thm. 2.4.1]
), whose proof will occupy the rest of the Section.

Theorem 6.1
.

The functor
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
of Theorem
4.28
has a pro-left derived functor
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
which factors through the stable
\infty
-category of effective log motives, giving rise to the log motivic Albanese functor (still denoted
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
):

L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}),

which is a pro-left adjoint of the functor

\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})\xrightarrow{L_{{\overline{\square}}}}\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}).

6.1.
Some preliminary results

We collect now some technical lemmas that will be used in the proof of the main theorem.

Recall the pair of adjoint functors from (
2.2.1
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\omega^{*}_{\log}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\textrm{d\'{e}t}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\colon\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}}}

where
\omega^{*}_{\log}F(X)=F(\underline{X}-|\partial X|)
. Recall that
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\omega^{*}_{\log}=\mathrm{id}
and that
by
[
BPØ22b
, Proposition 8.2.8]
, for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
we have that
\omega^{*}_{\log}(F)\cong\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaf
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
, we have an isomorphism:

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\cong\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}),

where
F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
denotes the maximal
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant quotient of
F
(see (
4.25.2
)).

Proof.

Consider the presentation
0\to L\to G\to F\to 0
of Proposition
4.24
.
Since
L\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
, we have that
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}L=\omega^{*}_{\log}L
and by Proposition
2.3
, by
[
BVK16
, Lemma 3.1.4]
and by Proposition
2.10
, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\omega^{*}_{\log}L)=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},L)=0\quad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\underline{Ext}}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\omega^{*}_{\log}L)=\operatorname{\underline{Ext}}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},L)=0.

Since
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
is exact, this gives an isomorphism

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}G)\cong\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)

Moreover, by
[
BPØ22b
, 8.2.4]
together with
[
MVW06
, Theorem 7.16]
,
\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}[0]\oplus\mathbb{Q}=M(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)
, so for all
i\geq 0

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\rm Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r})=\pi_{i}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\rm Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(M(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r})/\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r}.

By the fiber sequence
M(\mathbf{P}^{1},\mathrm{triv})\to M(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\oplus M(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)\to M(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)
and the fact that
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{G}_{a})
is both
(\mathbf{P}^{1},\mathrm{triv})
and
{\overline{\square}}
-local, we conclude that

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\rm Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r})=0\quad\textrm{for }i\geq 0.

We can now conclude from the sequence (
4.25.1
) and Proposition
2.3
, since we have that:

\omega_{\sharp}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}G)=\omega_{\sharp}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\omega^{*}_{\log}G^{sab})=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},G^{sab}).

∎

Let
\mathfrak{X}=(\overline{X},D)
be a smooth proper and geometrically integral modulus pair, and let
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{sab}
be the maximal semi-abelian quotient of
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
. Suppose that
X=\overline{X}\setminus D
has a
k
-rational point. Then, by Proposition
3.23
,
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{sab}
agrees with Serre’s semi-abelian Albanese variety of
X
,
\operatorname{Alb}_{X}
. We have an exact sequence as in Remark
4.25
:

(6.2.1)

0\to U(\mathfrak{X})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{sab}=\operatorname{Alb}_{X}\to 0

where
U(\mathfrak{X})
is a finite dimensions
k
-vector space.
Since
\operatorname{Alb}_{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
, which is a Serre subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
, the natural surjection of (
6.2.1
) (with rational coefficients) gives then rise to a map

(6.2.2)

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\operatorname{Alb}_{X},\mathbf{G}_{m})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\mathbf{G}_{m}).

Lemma 6.3
.

The map (
6.2.2
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We need to show that

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(U,\mathbf{G}_{m})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(U,\mathbf{G}_{m})=0.

which by Proposition
5.11
follow form analogous vanishing in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
, which are well-known (see e.g.
[
Ser75
, VII, Proposition 7]
).
∎

Notice that for every
G\in\mathbf{logCI}
, we have that
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},G)\in\mathbf{logCI}

since

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},G)=\pi_{0}(R\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},G))

and
R\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},G)
is clearly
{\overline{\square}}
-local (see
[
BM21
, Lemma 2.10]
).

Lemma 6.4
.

For any finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaf
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
, we have an isomorphism:

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\omega^{*}_{\log}\big(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\big).

where
\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}
is the tensor product of the category
\mathbf{logCI}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.2
, we have that

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\cong\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}).

Moreover, the right-hand side is in
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, hence by (
2.12.1
) we have an isomorphism

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}),

so we conclude since by Lemma
2.7
we have that

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\omega^{*}_{\log}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}\big(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\big).

∎

Remark 6.5
.

Thanks to Proposition
4.24
, we can write every
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
as filtered colimit of finitely presented
1
-reciprocity sheaves,
F=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}F_{i}
. Since the functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
from (
6.0.1
) commutes with all colimits, we have

\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F_{i}
. By the isomorphism

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\cong\mathrm{coker}(\mathbb{Q}\to h_{0}^{\log}({\mathbf{P}^{1}},[0]+[\infty])),

given for example by
[
BPØ22b
, 8.2.4]
together with
[
MVW06
, Theorem 7.16]
, we see that
\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}
is a compact object in
\mathbf{logCI}
, hence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
. By Lemma
6.4
we have an isomorphism

(6.5.1)

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\omega^{*}_{\log}\big(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\big).

Moreover, since
\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}
commutes with (filtered) colimits, we have that

(6.5.2)

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\bigl(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\big)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\big(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\big).

In particular, since
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
is closed under colimits we have that for exery
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
,

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\bigl(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\big)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, then:

(6.6.1)

H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F))=0\quad\textrm{for }j>1.

Proof.

Recall that for a torus
T
, we have that

(6.6.2)

H^{j}_{\rm Nis}(X^{\circ},T)=H^{j}_{\rm Zar}(X^{\circ},T)=0\quad\textrm{for }j>1.

By Proposition
4.24
, let
F=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}F_{i}
with
F_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
. Since
{\operatorname{dNis}}
-cohomology commutes with filtered colimits, the left hand side of (
6.6.1
) is isomorphic to

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}\big(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\big)).

The sheaf
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})
is a
0
-motivic homotopy invariant sheaf by
[
ABV09
, Corollary 1.3.9]
, hence
T_{i}:=\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})
is a torus. Then since
\omega^{*}_{\log}
is exact and preserves injective sheaves, we have by (
6.6.2
) that

H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}T_{i})=H^{j}_{\rm Nis}(X^{\circ},T_{i})=0\quad\textrm{for }j>1.

∎

Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
and
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
. For
H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
, consider the composition:

(6.6.3)

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},H)\xrightarrow{(1)}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbf{logCI}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}),\omega^{*}_{\log}(H))\xrightarrow{(2)}H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}(H))

where
(1)
is the application of the exact functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}
and the isomorphism (
2.12.1
) and
(2)
is given by the map
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
For a smooth scheme
X
, let
{\rm NS}^{r}(X)
be the group of codimension
r
-cycles modulo algebraic equivalence.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
and let
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
connected such that
X^{\circ}
is affine and
{\rm NS}^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{k}})=0
. Then for
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Comp}}(X)
the map
\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}(\operatorname{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}})
induces an isomorphism:

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F))))\simeq\\
H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F))).

Proof.

Let
F=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F_{i}
with
F_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
and
T=\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)
and
T_{i}=\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F_{i})
.
By Corollary
5.4
,
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a compact object in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
. Since filtered colimits are exact and
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
is closed under extensions, by (
6.5.2
) we have that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}T)\cong\\
\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}T_{i}).

On the other hand, by Lemma
6.4

we have

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,T)\cong\\
\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}))).

Moreover, since
\omega^{*}_{\log}
is exact and preserves injective sheaves we have that

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})))\cong H^{1}_{\rm Nis}(X^{\circ},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})).

Finally,
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})
is a lattice by
[
ABV09
, Corollary 1.3.9]
, hence it is enough to show that for every lattice
L
we have an isomorphism

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}L)\cong H^{1}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(X^{\circ},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}L).

By
[
ABV09
, Lemma 2.4.5]
, since
X^{\circ}
is affine and
{\rm NS}^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{k}})=0
, we have that

H^{1}_{\rm Nis}(X^{\circ},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\operatorname{Alb}_{X^{\circ}},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}L),

so it is enough to show that the canonical map
\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\mathbf{Alb}_{X^{\circ}}
induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{X^{\circ}},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}L).

If
k
is algebraically closed, we have
L\cong\mathbb{Q}^{r}
, hence the above isomorphism comes from Lemma
6.3
.
A Galois descent argument (see
[
ABV09
, Lemma 2.4.5, Step 1]
) allows us to deduce the isomorphism above for any
k
. ∎

6.2.
Deriving the Albanese functor

We are ready to prove Theorem
6.1
. The categories
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
are Grothendieck abelian categories and the functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}_{\log}
from (
6.0.1
) is exact and commutes with filtered colimits.

The derived
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})
is equivalent by classical reason to the
\infty
-category underlying the model category
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Cpx}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda))
with the
{\operatorname{dNis}}
-local model structure considered in
[
BM21
]
. In particular, by
[
BM21
, Lemma 2.15]
, the functor
i_{\mathrm{ltr}}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})
preserves filtered colimtis. In particular, the commutative square of
\infty
-categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\leq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{ltr}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})}

satisfies the hypotheses of
A.4
, so that
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})
has a pro-left adjoint
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
. We consider the
BC
-admissibility with respect to this diagram.
Recall from Lemma
A.14
that a compact object
P\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
is
BC
-admissible as an object of
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}})

if and only if for every injective object
I\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(P,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))=0\textrm{ for }i\neq 0.

We make the following definition (see
[
ABV09
, Def. 2.4.2]
):

Definition 6.8
.

X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
is
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial
if
X^{\circ}
is affine,
{\rm NS}^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{k}})=0
and

H^{j}_{\rm Zar}(\underline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}})=0\quad\text{ for }j>0.

Remark 6.9
.

If
\underline{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(R)
is affine and
\partial X
is supported on a principal divisor with global equation
f
, then
X^{\circ}=\operatorname{Spec}(R[\frac{1}{f}])
is affine, in particular if
{\rm NS}^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{k}})=0
we have that
X
is
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial and
X^{\circ}
satisfies the hypotheses of
[
ABV09
, Proposition 2.4.4]
.

The main technical input of the proof of Theorem
6.1
is the following result:

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}(k)
be
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial, then the complex
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)[0]
is
BC
-admissible.

Proof.

We follow (with some modifications) the path of the proof of
[
ABV09
, Proposition 2.4.4]
.
Since
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)[0]
is a compact object, by Lemma
A.14
it is enough to prove that

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{ltr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))=0,\quad\text{for $i>0$ and for every }I\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}\text{ injective.}

By
[
BPØ22b
, Proposition 4.3.2]
the
\operatorname{Ext}
groups in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
can be computed as cohomology groups:

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))=H^{i}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I)),

so we need to check that
H^{i}_{\rm dNis}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))=0
for
i>0
. In order to control this cohomology, we look then at the adjunction map

(6.10.1)

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I)

By Lemma
6.6
and Lemma
6.11
below, we get that
H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))=0
for
j>1
and that we have a surjection

(6.10.2)

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I)))\to H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))\to 0.

We are then left to show that the displayed morphism in (
6.10.2
) is the zero map.

For every modulus pair
\mathfrak{X}\in\textbf{Comp}(X)
, the canonical map
\mathbb{Q}_{tr}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}}
gives for any
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
a natural map (again we are using the fact that
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
is exact):

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},F)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{ltr}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\to H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F),

hence, from (
6.10.2
) we get a commutative diagram (cfr. with the proof of
[
ABV09
, 2.4.4]
)

(6.10.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I))))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(I)).}

Since
I
is injective in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
, the term
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}},I)
is zero. On the other hand, the left vertical map is an isomorphism by Lemma
6.7
, which implies that (
6.10.2
) is indeed the zero map. This finishes the reduction of the proof of
6.10
to Lemma
6.11
.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
and let
N
and
Q
be respectively the kernel and the cokernel (computed in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}
) of the morphism

(6.11.1)

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F.

Then for
j>0
we have the following vanishing:

(6.11.2)

\displaystyle H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,N)=H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,Q)=0.

Proof.

Since cohomology commutes with filtered colimits, let
F=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F_{i}
with
F_{i}
finitely generated
1
-reciprocity sheaves. For all
i
, let
K_{i}
and
N_{i}
be the kernel and the cokernel of the adjunction map

(6.11.3)

\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F_{i})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F_{i}.

As observed in Remark
6.5
, since filtered colimits are exact we have that
K=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}K_{i}
and
Q=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}Q_{i}
, hence it is enough to show that for all
i
:

(6.11.4)

\displaystyle H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,N_{i})=0,\quad j>0

(6.11.5)

\displaystyle H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,Q_{i})=0,\quad j>0.

By Lemma
6.4
we have that the left hand side of (
6.11.3
) is isomorphic to
\omega^{*}_{\log}\bigl(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\bigr)
.
Let
K_{i}
and
R_{i}
be the kernel and the cokernel of the adjunction map

\psi_{i}:\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\to F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}.

We have the following diagram:

(6.11.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\mathbf{logCI}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\omega^{*}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}F_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}_{\log}K_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}_{\log}\big(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}_{\log}F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}_{\log}R_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}\psi_{i}}

By the cancellation theorem
[
Voe10
]
,

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}M)\cong M
for
M\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
,
and
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\_)
is exact as an endo-functor on
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
.
Hence we get

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},K_{i})=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},R_{i})=0
,
in particular the sheaves
K_{i}
and
R_{i}
are birational sheaves in the sense of
[
KS17
]
(see
[
KS17
, Proposition 2.5.2]
).
In particular, since
\omega^{*}_{\log}
is exact and preserves injectives, by
[
MVW06
, Proposition 14.23]
and
[
KS17
, Proposition 2.3.3]
that

(6.11.7)

\displaystyle H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}K_{i})=H^{j}_{\rm Nis}(X^{\circ},K_{i})=0,\quad j>0

(6.11.8)

\displaystyle H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}R_{i})=H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(X^{\circ},R_{i})=0,\quad j>0.

Since
N_{i}
is a subsheaf of
\omega^{*}_{\log}K_{i}
,

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}N_{i}
is a subsheaf of
K_{i}
, so it is a birational sheaf, in particular it is an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
by
[
KS17
, Proposition 2.3.3 (a)]
so
N_{i}\cong\omega^{*}_{\log}\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}N_{i}
by (
2.12.1
).
Therefore the same argument gives the vanishing (
6.11.4
).

Let
H_{i}
be the kernel of the map
F_{i}\to F_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
. By a snake lemma argument on (
4.25.2
), there exists a lattice
L_{i}^{\prime}
and
r_{i}\geq 0
such that
\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r_{i}}/L_{i}^{\prime}\cong H_{i}
. By the exactness of
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
, we have that
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r_{i}}/\omega^{*}L^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}H_{i}
. Since
L^{\prime}
is a lattice,
H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}L^{\prime})=H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(X^{\circ},L^{\prime})=0
for
j>0
and by
[
RS21
, Corollary 6.8]
with
q=0

we have that (see (
2.1.2
)):

(6.11.9)

H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a})=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{Y\in X_{\operatorname{div}}^{\mathbf{Sm}}}H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{Y}}).

By the comparison of Zariski cohomology with Nisnevich cohomology for coherent sheaves we have that for all
Y\in X_{\operatorname{div}}^{\mathbf{Sm}}
:

H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{Y}})\cong H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\underline{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{Y}}).

By definition the map
\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
is the composition of blowups in smooth centers, hence the well know blow-up formula (see e.g.
[
Gro85
, Corollary IV.1.1.11]
) implies:

H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\underline{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{Y}})\cong H^{j}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\underline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}})=0,\;\;j>0,

where the last vanishing comes from the fact that
X
was taken
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial. In particular, we conclude that

(6.11.10)

H^{i}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}H_{i})=0\;\text{ for }i\not=0.

From the diagram (
6.11.6
) and a snake lemma argument, we get the following short exact sequence:

(6.11.11)

0\to\omega^{*}_{\log}K_{i}/N_{i}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}H_{i}\to\mathrm{ker}(Q_{i}\to\omega^{*}_{\log}R_{i})\to 0.

Now by (
6.11.7
) and (
6.11.4
) we have that
H^{j}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}(X,\omega^{*}_{\log}K_{i}/N_{i})=0
for
j>0
, so by (
6.11.10
), (
6.11.8
) and (
6.11.11
) we deduce (
6.11.5
).
∎

Given Proposition
6.10
, we can show the following

Lemma 6.12
.

The category
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is generated by the set of
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

The category
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is compactly generated, and a set of compact generators is given by
\mathbb{Q}_{\rm ltr}(X)[i]
, for
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Proposition
6.10
, it is then enough to show that any
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}
can be covered (even Zariski-locally) by
X_{i}\in\mathbf{SmlSm}
which are
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial. Let
\underline{U_{i}}
be a Zariski cover of
\underline{X}
such that
|\partial X|_{|\underline{U_{i}}}
is principal. By
[
ABV09
, Corollary 2.4.6]
), we can cover each
\underline{U_{i}}
by affine
\underline{U_{ij}}
such that
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}((\underline{U_{ij}})_{\overline{k}})=0
, and since
|\partial X|_{\underline{U_{i}}}
is principal,
|\partial X|_{\underline{U_{ij}}}
is again principal. Considering the log schemes
U_{ij}:=(\underline{U_{ij}},\partial X_{|U_{ij}})
, we have that
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}(\underline{(U_{ij}})_{\overline{k}})\to{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}((U_{ij}^{\circ})_{\overline{k}})
is surjective by
[
Ful98
, Example 10.3.4]
, hence
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}((U_{ij}^{\circ})_{\overline{k}})=0
. We conclude that
\{U_{ij}\}
is a Zariski cover of
X
by
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial log schemes.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

From Lemma
6.12
and Proposition
6.10
, we have that the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})))
is generated by a set of compact
BC
-admissible objects concentrated in degree zero. The existence of the derived log Albanese functor
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
as pro-left derived functor of
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
follows then from Theorem
A.13
, and by constrution it is equivalent to the pro-left adjoint of the functor
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})
.

We are left to show that the functor
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
factors through the localization

L_{{\overline{\square}}}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}).

Recall that
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is obtained as localization of
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{dNis}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
with respect to the class of maps:

(CI)

\mathbb{Q}_{\rm ltr}(X\times{\overline{\square}})[n]\to\mathbb{Q}_{\rm ltr}(X)[n]

for
X
in
\mathbf{SmlSm}(k)
. From the proof of Lemma
6.12
, we can suppose that
X
is
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial (this is the exact analogue of
[
ABV09
, 2.4.1]
). We are then left to show that
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\mathbb{Q}_{\rm ltr}(X\times{\overline{\square}})\to\mathbb{Q}_{\rm ltr}(X))
is contractible for
X
an
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial object. Since
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is
BC
-admissible by Proposition
6.10
, we have by Remark
A.9
that

L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\mathbb{Q}_{\rm tr}(X)[n])=``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i}"\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(i)}}[n],\quad\textrm{for any choice of }\mathfrak{X}\in\mathbf{Comp}(X).

Note also that if
X
is
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
-trivial, so is
X\times{\overline{\square}}
. Indeed,
(X\times{\overline{\square}})^{\circ}=X^{\circ}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}
is affine if
X^{\circ}
is affine,
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}((X^{\circ}\times\mathbf{A}^{1})_{\overline{k}})\cong{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{k}})
and
H^{i}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\underline{X}\times\mathbf{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}})\cong H^{i}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\underline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}})
for all
i
. Therefore

L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\mathbb{Q}_{\rm ltr}(X\times{\overline{\square}})[n])=``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i}"\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(i)}\times{\overline{\square}}},\quad\textrm{for any choice of }\mathfrak{X}\in\mathbf{Comp}(X).

On the other hand, by construction we have

\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(i)}\times{\overline{\square}}}\cong\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{X}^{(i)}},

proving the factorization. The pro-adjunction now is formal since for
X\in\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
, we have that
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})(X)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{logCI})
, hence it is
({\operatorname{dNis}},{\overline{\square}})
-local by
[
BM21
, Corollary 5.5]
, so:

\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})(X)\simeq i_{{\overline{\square}}}L_{{\overline{\square}}}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})(X)\simeq i_{{\overline{\square}}}\omega_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}(X),

hence for any
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(Y,\omega_{\leq 1}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}(X))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(i_{{\overline{\square}}}Y,\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}})(X))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(Y),X)

as required.
∎

6.3.
Some computations

Recall from
[
BVK16
, Theorem 9.2.3]
(see also
[
Par21
, Theorem 1.1]
for a statement in a language more similar to ours) that

(6.12.1)

L\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))\simeq\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))\oplus{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X))[1],

where
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X))
is the torus dual to the Néron-Severi. The goal of this section is to give an explicit description of
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)
: for
X=(\underline{X},\partial X)
, recall that
\omega(X)
is defined as
\underline{X}-|\partial X|
. We will prove the following result:

Theorem 6.13
.

Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}
geometrically connected and
(\overline{X},D)
a Cartier compactification of
X
. Then we have that

L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\cong\begin{cases}``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"(H^{i}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(nD))^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a})&\textrm{for }2\leq i\leq\dim(X)\\
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"\Bigl((H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(nD))/H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}))^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}\Bigr)\oplus{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X))&\textrm{for }i=1\\
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)&\textrm{for }i=0\\
0&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Moreover, the canonical map
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\to\oplus_{i=0}^{\dim(X)}L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)[i]
is an equivalence.

We start with the following observation:

Proposition 6.14
.

The inclusion
\mathcal{D}(i_{\mathbf{A}^{1}})\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})\to\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
has a left adjoint
L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}
such that
\pi_{i}(L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1})(F)=h_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
(the Suslin hyperhomology).

Proof.

By Proposition
5.11
, the inclusion
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})\to\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is fully faithful, then for
F\in\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
and
H\in\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(F,\mathcal{D}(i_{\mathbf{A}^{1}})(H))\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(F,\mathcal{D}(i_{\mathbf{A}^{1}})(H))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{DM}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F),H)\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(L\operatorname{Alb}C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F),H)

which implies that
L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}
exists and it coincides with
L\operatorname{Alb}C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
.
We can write
F=\operatorname{hocolim}_{i,n}F_{i}[n]
with
F_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}^{\star}
.
Since
C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathcal{DM}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

commutes with all (homotopy) colimits as a left adjoint,
we have

C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)\simeq\operatorname{hocolim}C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F_{i}[n])\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\operatorname{hocolim}(h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F_{i})[n]),

where
(*)
follows from Remark
4.25
.
Hence the homotopy groups of
C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
are
1
-motivic so that
\pi_{i}L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}(F)=\pi_{i}L\operatorname{Alb}C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)=\pi_{i}C_{*}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)=h_{i}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
. ∎

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}\colon\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})\to\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
. For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
geometrically connected,
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)
is a constant pro-object and

\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\simeq c(L\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))),

where
c
denotes the constant pro-object.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y_{\bullet})\to\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
be a resolution in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
by
\operatorname{Alb}
-trivial objects. Then
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)=\pi_{i}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(Y_{\bullet}))
. On the other hand, by construction,
\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\omega(Y_{\bullet}))\to\mathbb{Q}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\omega(X))

is a resolution in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
by affine
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{1}
-local objects in the sense of
[
ABV09
]
, so
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))=\pi_{i}\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(Y_{\bullet}))
. By Proposition
3.23
and (
6.2.1
),
for
\mathfrak{Y}_{\bullet}
Cartier compactifications of
Y_{\bullet}
, we have a fiber-cofiber sequence:

(6.15.1)

``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"U(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{\bullet})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}\to\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(Y_{\bullet})\to c(\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(Y_{\bullet}))),

where
U(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{\bullet})
are finite dimensional
k
-vector spaces. The complex
\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(Y_{\bullet}))
is
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-local, so we have that

\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}c(\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(Y_{\bullet})))\simeq c(\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(Y_{\bullet})))=c(L\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X)))

and for all
i,n
, there exist
r_{i,n}
such that
U(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{i})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}=\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r_{i,n}}
,
so we conclude since:

\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}(``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{\bullet}))=``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}^{\leq 1}(U(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{\bullet}))=0.

∎

The lemma above gives a natural map
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\to cL\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))
. Let

(6.15.2)

\textrm{Fib}(X):=\mathrm{hofib}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\to cL\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))).

In view of (
6.15.1
), we have that

(6.15.3)

\textrm{Fib}(X)\simeq``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"U(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{\bullet})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}.

since for
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y_{\bullet})\to\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
an
\operatorname{Alb}
-trivial resolution and
\mathfrak{Y}_{\bullet}
a Cartier compactification of
Y_{\bullet}
, the map
\mathbf{Alb}(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{i})\to\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(Y_{i}))
is surjective with kernel
U(\mathfrak{Y}^{(n)}_{i})
for all
i
.

Definition 6.16
.

Let
\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle\subseteq\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
be the stable
\infty
-subcategory generated by direct sums of
\mathbf{G}_{a}
and let
\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle^{\omega}
be the full subcategory of compact objects. In particular,
F\in\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle^{\omega}
if and only if there exist
s\leq t\in\mathbb{Z}
and
r_{s},\ldots,r_{t}\geq 0
such that

F\simeq\bigoplus_{i=s}^{t}\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r_{i}}[i].

Remark 6.17
.

Let
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}(\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle^{\omega})\subseteq\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
, then for all
X
as above
\textrm{Fib}(X)\in\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}(\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle^{\omega})

Remark 6.18
.

Let
\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}
be the category of finite dimensional
k
-vector spaces. By Proposition
5.11
and
[
Ser75
, VII, n.7, Proposition 8]
:

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r},\mathbf{G}_{a}^{s}[i])\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}}(k^{r},k^{s})[i]\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd})}(k^{r},k^{s}[i]).

In particular, the functor
V\mapsto\operatorname{Spec}(k[V^{\vee}])
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\_\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd})\to\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle^{\omega}

with quasi inverse given by
R\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\_)
.

Let
(\_)^{\vee}\colon\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}\to\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}^{\rm op}
denote the equivalence given by the dual vector space. It induces an equivalence:

(\_)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd})\to{\operatorname{Ind}}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}^{\rm op}).

Remark 6.19
.

There is a commutative diagram of stable
\infty
-category:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\langle\mathbf{G}_{a}\rangle^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect_{\rm fd}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ind}}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}^{\rm op})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}R\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\_)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(\_,\mathbf{G}_{a})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\_)^{\vee}}

where
\operatorname{Map}
denotes the mapping space in
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
.
This easily follows from the fact that any map of sheaves
f\colon\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r}\to\mathbf{G}_{a}^{s}
is indeed a map of vector groups, hence since
\mathbf{G}_{a}=k\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}
, we have a commutative diagram in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{s},\mathbf{G}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\mathbf{G}_{a}^{s})^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r},\mathbf{G}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\mathbf{G}_{a}^{r})^{\vee}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(k)^{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
geometrically connected. Then for any
(\overline{X},D)
Cartier compactification of
\underline{X}
and
i\geq 2
, we have that

L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\simeq``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"(H^{i}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(nD))^{\vee})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}.

In particular,
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)=0
for
i\geq\max(\dim(X),2)
.

Proof.

By (
6.12.1
), for
X
as above we have that for
i\geq 2

L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\simeq\pi_{i}\textrm{Fib}(X).

By Remark
6.19
we have that

(6.20.1)

\textrm{Fib}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Map}(\textrm{Fib}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}.

where for
V=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}V_{i}\in{\operatorname{Ind}}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}^{\rm op})
,
V^{\vee}=\mathrm{``lim"}V_{i}^{\vee}
. In particular, it is enough to compute
\pi_{-i}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(\textrm{Fib}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})
for
i\geq 2
, which again by (
6.12.1
) agree with
\pi_{-i}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})
.
By Theorem
6.1
and
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 9.7.1]
and
(
2.13.3
).

(6.20.2)

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}}(M(X),\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq R\Gamma(\underline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}}).

Let now
(\overline{X},D)
be a Cartier compactification of
\underline{X}
. This gives an isomorphism:

R\Gamma(\underline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}R\Gamma(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(nD)).

and the right-hand side is in
{\operatorname{Ind}}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\rm fd}^{\rm op})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.13
.

The only case left is
i=1
: we consider the long exact sequence of homotopy groups arising from (
6.15.2
). The map
L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))\to\pi_{0}(\textrm{Fib}(X))
is zero
since by (
6.12.1
),
L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))
is a torus, so
we get a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
:

(6.20.3)

0\to\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(X)\to L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\to L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))\overset{\eqref{eq;LAlb-usual}}{\cong}c({\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X)))\to 0.

Since
\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(X)=\mathrm{``lim"}V_{i}
with
V_{i}
vector groups, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}({\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X)),\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(X))=\pi_{1}\bigl(\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}({\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X),V))\bigr)

Since
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X))
is a torus, by
[
Ser75
, VII, n. 6, Proposition 7]
and Proposition
5.6
, we have that for all vector groups
V

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}({\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X),V))\simeq 0,

so (
6.20.3
) above splits and

(6.20.4)

L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\cong\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(X)\oplus{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X)).

Moreover, we have

\operatorname{Map}(\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\operatorname{Map}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})[0],

where
\operatorname{Map}
denotes the mapping space in
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
.
Hence (
6.20.4
) gives:

(6.20.5)

\pi_{1}L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\cong(\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})^{\vee})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}\oplus{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\omega(X)).

Let us compute
\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})
: by (
6.20.2
) and the degeneration of (
5.12.1
) we have an exact sequence:

(6.20.6)

0\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})\to H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\to\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})\to 0.

In particular, we have a similar exact sequence for the log scheme
(\overline{X},\mathrm{triv})
, which we now investigate. For
\overline{X}
proper we have by construction (see Theorem
4.28
) that
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X})
is the constant pro-object
\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X})
, so there is a surjective map

\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X})

whose kernel is an extension of the torus
T:=\mathrm{ker}(\operatorname{Alb}(\omega(X))\to\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}))
by the pro-vector group
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"U({\overline{X}},nD)\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}
, where
U({\overline{X}},nD)
comes from Definition
3.6
.
For
i\geq 1
, we have that
