\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(U({\overline{X}},nD),\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
and
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(T,\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
, so we have a surjective map:

(6.20.7)

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a}).

Combining (
6.20.6
), and (
6.20.7
) we have a commutative diagram with exact rows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

so to conclude it is enough to show that
L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X})\cong{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\overline{X})
, which implies that
\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
, so the diagram above implies

\operatorname{Hom}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X),\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\mathrm{coker}(H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})\to H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}))

and we will conclude by duality and (
6.20.5
).

By (
6.20.4
), it is enough to show that
\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(\overline{X})=0
. By (
6.20.1
), we have

\pi_{1}\textrm{Fib}(\overline{X})\cong\pi_{1}(\operatorname{Map}(\textrm{Fib}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong(\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(\textrm{Fib}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}),

so it is enough to show that
\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(\textrm{Fib}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
. By (
6.15.2
) for
\overline{X}
we have a fiber-cofiber sequence:

(6.20.8)

\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\to\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\to\operatorname{Map}(\textrm{Fib}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a}).

By (
6.12.1
), we have

\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}({\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\oplus\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a}).

Since
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\overline{X})
is a torus and
\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X})
is an abelian variety, we have:

\operatorname{Map}({\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq 0\quad\textrm{and}\quad\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}),

where the last isomorphism is classical (see
[
Ser75
, VII, n.17, Théorème 7]
), and

\pi_{-2}\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})=0.

Finally,
\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathbf{G}_{a})

by (
6.20.2
), so the map

\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\to\pi_{-1}\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{X}),\mathbf{G}_{a})

in the long exact sequence of homotopy groups of (
6.20.8
) is an isomorphism, which implies the desired vanishing.
∎

Remark 6.21
.

We observe from Theorem
6.13
two extreme cases: if
X
is affine, we have that

H^{i}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\simeq\underset{n}{\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}}H^{i}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{X}(nD))=0
for
i\geq 1
, so

L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)=\begin{cases}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)&\textrm{if }i=0\\
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(X)&\textrm{if }i=1\\
0&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In particular,
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)
is constant for
i\geq 1
.
For
X
proper,
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong H^{1}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}})
, so

L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)=\begin{cases}\operatorname{Alb}(X)&\textrm{if }i=0\\
{\operatorname{\mathrm{NS}}}^{*}(X)&\textrm{if }i=1\\
(H^{i}(\overline{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{G}_{a}&\textrm{if }i\geq 2.\end{cases}

In this case,
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X)
is a constant pro-object. This shows that Proposition
7.4
cannot be extended to the whole
\mathcal{DM}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
: in general, if
M\in\mathcal{DM}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
,
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{*}M)
is not equal to
L\operatorname{Alb}(M)
: the difference is controlled by coherent cohomology of degree
\geq 2
.

6.4.
Open questions

We end this Section with the following observation. It seems to be an interesting question to determine under which conditions
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
is a constant pro-object.

This is related to the following problem: let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a desingularisation of a
d
-dimensional, integral variety over a field
k
, and let
D
be an effective Cartier divisor on
X^{\prime}
covering the exceptional fibre, and assume that
{\operatorname{codim}}_{X}(\pi(D))\geq 2
. Let
rD
denote the
r
-th infinitesimal thickening of
D
and
F^{d}K_{0}(X^{\prime},rD)
the subgroup of the relative
K
-group
K_{0}(X^{\prime},rD)
generated by the cycle classes of closed points of
X^{\prime}-|D|
, for each
r\geq 1
.

Bloch and Srinivas conjectured (see
[
Sri85
, p. 6]
) that the pro-object
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}"F^{d}K_{0}(X^{\prime},nD)
is essentially constant and equal to
F^{d}K_{0}(X)
. The Bloch–Srinivas conjecture was proved for normal surfaces by Krishna–Srinivas
[
KS02
, Theorem 1.1]
, and for
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
it was later extended to higher dimensional projective and affine varieties over an algebraically closed field by Krishna
[
Kri06
, Theorem 1.1]

[
Kri10
, Thorem 1.2]
and Morrow
[
Mor15
, Theorem 0.1,
(iii), (iv)
]
6
6

6

The conjecture is indeed true in a more general class of examples: the interested reader can check
[
Mor15
, Theorem 0.1
(i)-(vii)
]
.

The proof of
[
Mor15
]
indeed relies on a natural reformulation of the Bloch–Srinivas conjecture for the Chow groups with modulus:

Theorem 6.22

(cfr.
[
Mor15
, Theorem 0.3]
)
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero and
\pi\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and
D
be as above and assume that
X
is projective. Then the pro-object
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"_{n}{\operatorname{CH}}_{0}(X,nD)
is constant with stable value equal to the Levine–Weibel Chow group of zero cycles
{\operatorname{CH}}^{LW}_{0}(Y)
of
[
LW85
]
(see also
[
BK18
]
).

By the universal property of the Albanese map, we deduce that if in the situation of Theorem
6.22
we assume that
D
is a simple normal crossing divisor, the pro-object
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"_{n}\mathbf{Alb}_{(X^{\prime},nD)}
is indeed essentially constant, so the pro-object

L_{0}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(X-\pi(|D|),\mathrm{triv})
is essentially constant.
Then the following question arises naturally:

Question 6.23
.

Let
X
be a proper variety and
U\subset X
be a smooth open subvariety. When is the pro-reciprocity sheaf
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{log}(M(U,\mathrm{triv}))
essentially constant?

Notice that in general
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{log}(M(U,\mathrm{triv}))
is not essentially constant: let
X
be a proper non-singular surface, and
U=X-Y
for some closed subscheme
Y
of
X
. Let
(X^{\prime},D)
be the blow-up of
X
in
Y
. As observed in
[
Har68
, p. 407]
, if some irreducible component of
Y
is a point, then
\dim(H^{1}(U,\mathcal{O}_{U}))=\infty
. On the other hand,
\dim(H^{1}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(nD)))
is finite for every
n
, so
L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{log}(M(U,\mathrm{triv}))
is not constant by Theorem
1.3
. At the moment, we do not know if there is a nice family of pairs
U\subseteq X
that answers Question
6.23
positively.

7.
Logarithmic 1-motivic complexes

For any perfect field
k
and any commutative ring
\Lambda
, recall the stable
\infty
-category of
1
-motivic complexes
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\Lambda)
, i.e. the full stable
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
generated by
M(X)
, with
\dim(X)\leq 1
. If
\Lambda=\mathbb{Q}
, by
[
ABV09
, Theorem 2.4.1]
, the composition

L\operatorname{Alb}_{\leq 1}\colon\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{L\operatorname{Alb}}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

is an equivalence. To ease the notation, we will denote the functor
R\omega^{*}_{\log}
from (
2.6.1
) simply by:

\omega^{*}:\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}).

We give the following definition:

Definition 7.1
.

For any perfect field
k
and any commutative ring
\Lambda
, we let
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}_{\leq 1}}(k,\Lambda)
be the full stable
\infty
-subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
generated by
\omega^{*}\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(U)[n]
for a unipotent group scheme
U
. We will call it the stable
\infty
-category of
log
1
-motives
.

Remark 7.2
.

By
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 7.6.7]
, if
k
satisfies
(RS)
the
\infty
-category
\omega^{*}\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}
is equivalent to the
\infty
-subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
generated by
M(X)
with
{\underline{X}}
a proper smooth curve. Moreover, if
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
, every unipotent group scheme splits as a direct sum of
\mathbf{G}_{a}
, hence the categroy
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}_{\leq 1}}(k,\Lambda)
is generated by
\omega^{*}\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}[n]
. Moreover, if
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, the functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
from Theorem
6.1
factors through
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}_{\leq 1}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
: indeed the category
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is generated by
h_{0}(\mathfrak{C})[n]
with
\mathfrak{C}=(\overline{C},C_{\infty})
a proper modulus pair of dimension
1
such that
\overline{C}-|C_{\infty}|
is affine. By
[
RY16
]
and the proof of Proposition
3.18
, we have that
h_{0}(\mathfrak{C})\simeq\mathbf{Alb}_{\mathfrak{C}}
, so the exact sequence (
6.2.1
) gives a fiber sequence in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
:

\mathbf{G}_{a}^{\oplus r}[n]\to h_{0}(\mathfrak{C})[n]\to h_{0}(\mathfrak{C}_{red})[n].

Let
C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{SmlSm}}(k)
be the log scheme
(\overline{C},\partial C)
with
|\partial C|=|C_{\infty}|
, then

\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}h_{0}(\mathfrak{C}_{red})[m]=\omega^{*}h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(\overline{C}-C_{\infty})[m]=\omega^{*}M(\overline{C}-C_{\infty})[m]

where the last equality is
[
Voe00
, Thm. 3.4.2]
since
\overline{C}-C_{\infty}
is affine. By construction
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\mathbf{G}_{a}[n]=\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}[n]
. By repeating this argument backwards we conclude that the functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
is also essentially surjecrive on
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}_{\leq 1}}(k,\Lambda)
.

From now on, we consider again
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
and
\Lambda=\mathbb{Q}
. We have the following generalization of
[
ABV09
, Theorem 2.4.1]
:

Theorem 7.3
.

The composition
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}\circ\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
is equivalent to the constant pro-object functor. In particular, the functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
is fully faithful and induces an equivalence

\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\colon\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}_{\leq 1}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\colon\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}.

Proof.

By construction, the proof follows from Proposition
7.4
and Lemma
7.5
below.
∎

Proposition 7.4
.

There is a commutative diagram of stable
\infty
-cateogries:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\operatorname{Alb}_{\leq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}}

Proof.

The category
\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is generated by
M^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(C)
with
C
affine curve, and for such
C
we have
{\rm NS}^{1}(C_{\overline{k}})=0
. It is then enough to show that

L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{*}M^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(C))\simeq jL\operatorname{Alb}_{\leq 1}(M^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(C)).

Recall that
\omega^{*}M^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(C)=M(\overline{C},\partial C)
with
\overline{C}
any smooth compactification and
\partial C
is the log structure associated to
the closed subscheme
C_{\infty}:=\overline{C}\setminus C
with reduced structure. Since
C
is affine, the right-hand side is equivalent to the constant pro-object
\operatorname{Alb}(C)[0]
, so we conclude by Theorem
6.13
since there exists
n\gg 0
such that
H^{1}(\overline{C},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}(nC_{\infty}))=0
and
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\overline{C},\partial C)=\operatorname{Alb}(C)
.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

For all
i>0
,
L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a})=0
.

Proof.

For all
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
and all
i
, we have by adjunction that

(7.5.1)

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F[i])\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F)[i]).

Noting
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}\simeq L_{{\overline{\square}}}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{G}_{a})[0]

and
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{a}
by (
2.13.3
),
we get

(7.5.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}^{eff}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F)[i])

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(\omega^{\sharp}_{\log}\mathbf{G}_{a},\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F)[i])

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F)[i])

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(\mathbf{G}_{a},F[i]).

where the first equivalence holds since
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(F)[i]
is
{\overline{\square}}
-local, the second follows from (
2.13.2
) and the last holds since

\omega_{\sharp}^{\log}\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}\simeq id
.
By Theorem
5.12
, we have that

L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\bigoplus_{j}L_{j}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a})[j],

hence noting
\pi_{0}
commutes with products, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}(L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F[i])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}(\mathbf{G}_{a},F[i])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{j\geq 0}\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}(L_{j}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F[i-j])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{j=0}^{i}\operatorname{Ext}^{i-j}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(L_{j}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathbf{G}_{a},F),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where
(*)
follows from (
7.5.1
) and (
7.5.2
). So,
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{i}\operatorname{Alb}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F)
is a direct summand of
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathbf{G}_{a},F)
, which for
i\geq 2
is zero by Proposition
5.11
. For
i=1
, the above diagram gives

\displaystyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}^{{\operatorname{tr}}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathbf{G}_{a},F)}\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{0}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F)\oplus

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F).

On the other hand,
L_{0}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a})\cong\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{G}_{a}))\cong\mathbf{G}_{a}
since
L\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
is the derived functor of
\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{L}\textit{og}}_{\mathrm{d\acute{e}t}}
is fully faithful, so Proposition
5.9
and Corollary
5.4
implies that

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}}(L_{1}\operatorname{Alb}^{\log}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{G}_{a}),F)=0,

which concludes the proof.
∎

8.
Laumon
1
-motives and compact objects

In this Section, we combine the results of
[
Ber14
]
with some arguments of
[
BVK16
]
. As before, let
k
be a field of characteristic zero.

8.1.
Review of Laumon
1
-motives

The following definition is adapted from
[
Ber14
, 1]
.

Definition 8.1
.

An
effective Laumon
1
-motive
is a two-terms complex
M=[\Gamma\xrightarrow{u}G]
, where
\Gamma
is a formal
k
-group and
G
is a connected algebraic
k
-group, both seen as objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k)
. We say that
M
is
étale
if
\Gamma
is a lattice
7
7

7

Note that this definition is different from the one given in
[
BVB09
, 1.4]
, where the authors require in addition that
U(G)=0
. An
effective morphism

\textstyle{M=[\Gamma\xrightarrow{u}G]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f,g)}
\textstyle{M^{\prime}=[\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{u^{\prime}}G^{\prime}]}

is a map of complexes. We denote the category of effective (resp. étale) Laumon
1
-motives by
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{a,{\rm eff}}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,{\rm eff}}
). An effective Laumon
1
-motive is an
effective Deligne
1
-motive
if
G
is semi-abelian. We write
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{D,{\rm eff}}
for the full subcategory of effective Deligne
1
-motives.

Definition 8.2
.

An effective morphism
(f,g)\colon M\to M^{\prime}
is
strict
if
g
has (smooth) connected kernel, and a
quasi-isomorphism
if
g
is an isogeny,
f
is surjective and
\mathrm{ker}(f)=\mathrm{ker}(g)
is a finite
k
-group scheme.

Note that if
(f,g)
is strict and
g
is an isogeny, then
g
is an isomorphism of commutative algebraic groups. Write
\Sigma
for the class of quasi-isomorphisms: it admits a calculus of right fractions (see
[
BVK16
, C.2.4]
or
[
Ber13
, Lemma 1.6]
). We can now give the following Definition (see
[
Ber14
, Definition 2]
and
[
BVB09
, Definition 1.4.4]
).

Definition 8.3
.

The category of
étale Laumon
(resp.
Laumon
, resp
Deligne
)
1
-motives

\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{a}
, resp.
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{D}
) is the localization by
\Sigma
of the effective category.

Recall
[
BVK16
, Appendix B]
for the notion of
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
for an additive category
\mathcal{C}
. The proof of the following proposition is identical to
[
BVK16
, Corollary C.7.3]
:

Proposition 8.4
.

The categories
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{D}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
are abelian.

Definition 8.5

(see
[
Ber14
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}

whose objects are
1
-motives
M=[\Gamma\xrightarrow{u}G]
with
\mathrm{ker}(u)=0
.

Lemma 8.6
.

The category
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is a generating subcategory of
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, and it is closed under kernels and extensions. Moreover, for every object
M\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
there exists a monomorphism
f:M^{\prime}\to M^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
such that
M=\mathrm{coker}(f)
.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
Ber14
, Lemma 4]
.
∎

Remark 8.7
.

The reader might wonder if there are interesting examples of étale Laumon
1
-motives which are not Deligne
1
-motives. The prototype of such example is given by the
1
-motive
M^{\natural}=[\Gamma\to\mathbf{G}^{\natural}]
which is the universal
\mathbf{G}_{a}
-extension of the Deligne
1
-motive
[\Gamma\to G]
. Starting from the motive
M^{\natural}
it is possible to construct the universal
sharp
extension
M^{\sharp}
of
M
, as discussed in
[
BVB09
]
. Note however that the cateogry
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}
is not closed under
\sharp
-extensions: as remarked in
[
BVB09
, 3.1.5]
,
[0\to\mathbf{G}_{a}]^{\sharp}=[\widehat{\mathbf{G}}_{a}\to\mathbf{G}_{a}^{2}]
, which is clearly not étale.

Remark 8.8
.

The category of Deligne
1
-motives has an interesting self-duality, induced by the classical Cartier duality for algebraic groups. This extends to Laumon
1
-motives, see
[
BVB09
]
. Note that while the Cartier dual of a Deligne
1
-motive is again a Deligne
1
-motive, the dual of an étale Laumon
1
-motive is in general not étale. For example, if
A
is an Abelian variety (see as
1
-motive
[0\to A]
), its universal
\mathbf{G}_{a}
extension is the étale Laumon
1
-motive
A^{\natural}=[0\to A^{\natural}]
, which is not a Deligne
1
-motive. Its Cartier dual
(A^{\natural})^{*}
is the
1
-motive
[\widehat{A}^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}]
, where
A^{\prime}
is the dual Abelian variety of
A
and
\widehat{A}^{\prime}
is the formal completion of
A^{\prime}
along the identity. Clearly,
(A^{\natural})^{*}
is a Laumon
1
-motive that is not étale.

Remark 8.9
.

Consider the functor

\rho^{\rm eff}\colon\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,{\rm eff}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\quad[L\xrightarrow{u}G]\mapsto\mathrm{coker}(u)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}.

If
[L_{1}\xrightarrow{u_{1}}G_{1}]\to[L_{2}\xrightarrow{u_{2}}G_{2}]\in\Sigma
, then by definition
\mathrm{coker}(u_{1})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\cong\mathrm{coker}(u_{2})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. This together with
[
BVK16
, Lemma B.1.2]
implies that
\rho^{\rm eff}
induces:

\rho\colon\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}).

Lemma 8.10
.

The restriction of
\rho
to
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
induces an equivalence (cf. Def.
4.23
):

\rho^{\star}\colon\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}.

Proof.

By definition, for
[L\to G]\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
,
\rho([L\to G])\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
, and by Proposition
2.10
for every morphism
f
we have that
\rho(f)
is a map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
, hence
\rho^{\star}
is well defined. The presentation of Proposition
4.24
gives then a quasi-inverse of
\rho^{\star}
.
∎

Remark 8.11
.

For a category
\mathcal{C}
, we write
{\operatorname{Ind}}(\mathcal{C})
for the Ind-category of
\mathcal{C}
as in e.g.
[
KS06
]
. By
[
KS06
, Prop. 6.3.4]
and Remark
5.8
the functor
{\operatorname{Ind}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
induced by filtered colimits is fully faithful. It is also essentially surjective by Proposition
4.24
, hence it is an equivalence. Combining this with Lemma
8.6
and
8.10
, we have a functor

(8.11.1)

T\colon\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to{\operatorname{Ind}}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})\overset{(*)}{\simeq}{\operatorname{Ind}}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q})\simeq\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})

where
(*)
follows from the fact that
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is a generating subcategory of
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Since
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is abelian by Lemma
8.4
, it is idempotent-complete, hence following the steps of
[
KS06
, Exercise 6.1]
the functor (
8.11.1
) is fully faithful and it identifies
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
with a set of compact generators of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
. Moreover, by
[
KS06
, Proposition 8.6.11]
, the category
T(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
is closed under extensions in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}
.

8.2.
The derived category of étale Laumon
1
-motives

By
[
KS06
, Proposition 8.6.11]
and Remark
8.11
, the image of the functor
\rm T
of (
8.11.1
) is a Serre subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, hence we can consider the triangulated category
D^{b}_{\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
of bounded complexes of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
such that
H_{n}(C)=T([L_{n}\to G_{n}])
for
[L_{n}\to G_{n}]\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

Remark 8.12
.

The functor
T
of (
8.11.1
) induces an equivalence of triangulated categories:

D^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})\simeq D^{b}_{\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

where the latter is the triangulated derived category , since by Proposition
5.11
, every object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}^{\star}
is of projective dimension at most 1 in the sense of
[
Kel99
]
, in particular the image of
T
satisfies
[
Kel99
, 1.21 Lemma (c2)]
, hence the equivalence comes from
[
Kel99
, 1.21 Lemma (c)]
.

Definition 8.13
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
be the full
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
spanned by bounded complexes
C\in\mathcal{D}^{b}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
such that
\pi_{n}C=T([L_{n}\to G_{n}])
for
[L_{n}\to G_{n}]\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

Remark 8.14
.

Notice that since the category
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
does not have enough injective nor projective objects, we cannot use
[
Lur17
, 1.3]
to construct
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
directly.

Lemma 8.15
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories:

\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))^{\omega}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})

where the left hand side denotes the subcategory of compact objects as in
[
Lur09
, Notation 5.3.4.6]
.

Proof.

Since the set of objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})
lying in the image of (
8.11.1
) is a set of compact generators, by
[
Sta16
, Lemma 094B]
we have an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))^{\omega}\simeq{\rm Idem}(\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}))

where the right-hand side is the idempotent completion of
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
, see
[
Lur09
, Definition 5.1.4.1]
. On the other hand, the category
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
is idempotent-complete since the image of (
8.11.1
) is idempotent complete (it is an abelian subcategory), hence every object of
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q}))^{\omega}
lies in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
. The other inclusion is clear.
∎

Theorem 8.16
.

Let
\mathbf{log}{\mathcal{DM}}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1,{\rm gm}}(k,\mathbb{Q}):=\mathbf{log}{\mathcal{DM}}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\omega}
. The functor
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}
preserves compact objects and it induces an equivalence

\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{log}{\mathcal{DM}}^{\rm eff}_{\leq 1,{\rm gm}}(k,\mathbb{Q}).

Proof.

By Lemma
8.15
, if
C\in\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\leq 1})
is compact, then it is a bounded complex such that
\pi_{i}(C)=T(M_{i})
for
M_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. In particular, there exists
n
such that
C=\tau_{\geq n}C
, so we get a fiber-cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},\leq 1})
:

T(M_{n})[n]\to C\to\tau_{\geq n-1}C.

If
C
is compact, then
\tau_{\geq n-1}C
is compact, so by induction on the length of the bounded complex it is enough to show that for
M\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, the object
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(T(M)[n])
is compact. As observed in Lemma
8.6
, there is an exact sequence in
\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
:

0\to M^{\prime}\to M^{\prime\prime}\to M\to 0

with
M^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{1,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{a,\star}
, and since
T
is exact we have

\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(T(M)[n])={\rm cofib}(\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(T(M^{\prime})[n])\to\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(T(M^{\prime\prime})[n]).

Thus, it is enough to show that
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(T(M)[n])
is compact for
M=[L\overset{u}{\hookrightarrow}G]
. In this case, we have that
T(M)=\mathrm{coker}(u)
, so we have a cofiber sequence

\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(L[n])\to\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(G[n])\to\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(T(M)[n]).

We conclude since
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(L)[n]=\omega^{*}L[n]
and
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{logDM}^{eff}}}_{\leq 1}(G[n])=\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}_{\log}(G)[n]
are compact. The equivalence then follows from Theorem
7.3
and Lemma
8.15
.
∎

Appendix A
Pro-left derived functors

In this appendix we generalize to pro-left adjoints the results discussed in
[
ABV09
, 2.1]
and
[
KS06
, 14.3]
for left adjoints. We use in an essential way the formalism of (stable)
\infty
-categories of
[
Lur17
]
and
[
Lur09
]
.

A.1.

We consider the following commutative square:

(A.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}}

where
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
are
\infty
-categories,
L_{\mathcal{C}}
(resp.
L_{\mathcal{D}}
) has a fully faithful right adjoint
i_{\mathcal{C}}
(resp.
i_{\mathcal{D}}
). Let
\alpha:L_{\mathcal{D}}G\to G^{\prime}L_{\mathcal{C}}
be the equivalence that makes the diagram commute. As observed in
[
Lur17
, Definition 4.7.4.13]
,
\alpha
induces a natural transformation (the
Beck–Chevalley
map)

BC(\alpha):Gi_{\mathcal{C}}\to i_{\mathcal{D}}G^{\prime}.

Definition A.1
.

In the situation of
A.1
, an object
X
of
\mathcal{D}
is
BC(\alpha)
-admissible if and only if for any
Y\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
the Beck-Chevalley map induces an equivalence:

(A.1.1)

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}(X,i_{\mathcal{D}}G^{\prime}(Y))\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}(X,Gi_{\mathcal{C}}(Y)).

Assume that
G
and
G^{\prime}
have left adjoints
F
and
F^{\prime}
, then by adjunction
\alpha
induces a map

\beta\colon F^{\prime}L_{\mathcal{D}}\to L_{\mathcal{C}}F.

A.2.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an (arbitrary)
\infty
-category. We consider as in
[
Hoy18
, Section 2]
the
\infty
-category of pro-objects
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}

together with the “constant pro-object” embedding
c\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}(\mathcal{C})
such that
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}}(\_,c(Y))
commmutes with cofiltered limits.

Every element in
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}
is corepresented by a diagram
I\to\mathcal{C}
for
I
(the nerve of) a small cofiltered poset.
We will often denote an object of
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}
as
``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i\in I}"X_{i}
for a diagram
I\to\mathcal{C}
. By construction we have that

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}}(``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i\in I_{1}}"X_{i},``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{j\in I_{2}}"Y_{j})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{j}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(X_{i},Y_{j})

where the limits and colimits are computed in the
\infty
-category of spaces
\mathcal{S}
.

Remark A.2
.

The functors
L_{\mathcal{C}}
and
i_{\mathcal{C}}
extend levelwise to an adjunction
(\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathcal{C}},\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}i_{\mathcal{C}})
on
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}
and
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
with the same properties. The verification is immediate.
In particular, if
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
have all limits,
G
and
G^{\prime}
give the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}G^{\prime}}

which satisfies the hypotheses of Situation
A.1
with equivalence

\alpha^{pro}:L_{\mathcal{D}}\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}G\to\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}G^{\prime}\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}L_{\mathcal{C}}.

In particular, since
i_{\mathcal{D}}
commutes with all limits being a right adjoint, it is immediate that
X\in\mathcal{D}
is
BC(\alpha)
-admissible if and only if it is
BC(\alpha^{pro})
-admissible.

Remark A.3
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is an accessible stable
\infty
-category equipped with a
t
-structure
(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
with heart
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C}
is also stable (see e.g.
[
KST19
, Lemma 2.5]
) and it comes equipped with a
t
-structure such that
(\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
(resp.
(\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
) is the full subcategory of objects which are formal limits of objects in
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
(resp
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
).

A.3.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a Grothendieck abelian category. Write
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})
for the model category of chain complexes with the injective model structure. Let
W
be the class of quasi isomorphisms. We consider the
\infty
-categories (see
[
Lur17
, 1.3.5]
)
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})=N_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A}))
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})=N_{\mathrm{dg}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A}))[W^{-1}]
. An exact functor
G\colon\mathbf{A}\to\mathbf{B}
between Grothendieck abelian categories induces a
\mathrm{dg}
-functor
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(G):\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{B})
which preserves
W
, so by taking the
\mathrm{dg}
-nerve it induces a functor
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
such that
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(C)=\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(G)(C)
, and by e.g.
[
Hin20
, Proposition 4.3]
, it induces
\mathcal{D}(G)\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{B})
on the localizations. Note that both functors are clearly stable (i.e. they commute with shifts). By construction, we have a commutative square of
\infty
-categories:

(A.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{B})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathbf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathbf{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(G)}

where
L_{\mathbf{A}}
and
L_{\mathbf{B}}
have fully faithful right adjoints
i_{\mathbf{A}}
and
i_{\mathbf{B}}
. We will fix
\alpha
that makes (
A.3.1
) commute and just say that an object is
BC
-admissible.

Remark A.4
.

Note that, since
G
is exact, we can identify the Beck-Chevalley transformation
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)i_{\mathbf{A}}\to i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)
as follows. For any object
I\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})
(i.e. a fibrant complex in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})
for the injective model structure), the object
\mathcal{D}(G)(I)
is a fibrant replacement of
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathcal{A}}(I))
. The map
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}I)\to i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I)
in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{B})
is thus given by the functorial fibrant replacement. In particular, if
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(G)
is a right Quillen functor, the map
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}I)\to i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I)
is an equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
. On the other hand, the functors considered here are not necessarily right Quillen.

Remark A.5
.

If the functor
G
has a left adjoint
F
, then
X
is
BC
-admissible if and only if it is
F
-admissible in the sense of
[
ABV09
, Definition 2.1.5]
.

A.4.
Pro-left derived functors

Fix
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
G
as above and we assume that
G
and
i_{\mathbf{B}}
commute with filtered colimits. Since
G
is exact, it preserves finite limits, so it has a pro-left adjoint
F\colon\mathbf{B}\to\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}
. The functor
\mathcal{D}(G)
is then an accessible functor between presentable
\infty
-categories that preserves finite limits, hence it has a pro-left adjoint
LF\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{B})\to\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})
(see e.g.
[
Hoy18
, Remark 2.2]
).

For any chain complex
C
, let
\sigma_{\leq n}C
and
\sigma_{\geq n}C
denote the stupid truncations (see
[
Sta16
, Tag 0118]
)
8
8

8

Notice that we chose the convention for
chain
complexes, which is different from
[
ABV09
, Lemma 2.1.10]
: there the convention is for
cochain
complexes
. We have an equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}
:

(A.5.1)

C\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}\sigma_{\geq-m}\sigma_{\leq n}C).

Definition A.6
.

We say that a chain complex
C
is
strictly bounded
if there exists
m,n
such that
C=\sigma_{\leq n}C=\sigma_{\geq-m}C
.

Remark A.7
.

Notice that if
C\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{B})
, the object
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(C)
a priori lives in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A})
, which contains strictly
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})
. If
C
is a strictly bounded complex, let
m,n
such that
C=\sigma_{\leq n}C=\sigma_{\geq-m}C
, then for
r\in[-m,n]
let
F^{\heartsuit}(C_{r})=``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"_{i\in I_{r}}(X_{r})_{i}
, for small cofiltered posets
I_{r}
. Then one can find a cofinal set
I\subseteq I_{r}
for all
r
such that

\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(C)=``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i\in I}"(\ldots\to(X_{r})_{i}\to(X_{r-1})_{i}\to\ldots).

In particular,
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(C)\in\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A}))
.

Proposition A.8
.

For all
C\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
strictly bounded, there is an equivalence in
\mathcal{S}
:

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(C)[0],Y)\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(C[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(Y)).

Proof.

Let
C_{n}=0
for
n\notin[-r,s]
. The cofiber of the map
\sigma_{\leq s-1}C\to C
is equivalent to
C_{s}[s]
, hence by induction on
r+s
we are reduced to the case where
C=C_{s}[s]
with
C_{s}\in\mathcal{B}
, and clearly
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(C_{s}[s])=F(C_{s})[s]
.
Since
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})
is pointed, by
[
Lur17
, Remark 1.1.2.8]
it is enough to show that for all
m
,

\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})}(F(X)[m],Y)\simeq\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(X[m],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(Y)).

Let
F(X)=``\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}"T_{i}
, then for all
m
we have an isomorphism of abelian groups:

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{B})}(X[m],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(G)(Y))=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})}(T_{i}[m],Y),

and since
\pi_{0}
commutes with filtered colimits in
\mathcal{S}
we have by
[
Lur17
, Remark 1.3.1.5, Remark 1.3.1.11 and Definition 1.3.2.1]
:

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})}

\displaystyle(F(X)[n],Y)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{A})}(T_{i}[n],Y)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{coker}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})}(T_{i}[n+1],Y)\to\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})}(T_{i}[n],Y)).

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{coker}(\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{B})}(X[n+1],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(G)(Y))\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})}(X[n],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(G)(Y)))

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(X[n],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(Y)).

∎

Remark A.9
.

A priori, there is no relation between
F
and
LF
, but if
X
is strictly bounded and
BC
-admissible, then Proposition
A.8
implies that

LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}(X))\simeq L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(X)[0].

In particular for
X\in\mathbf{B}
such that
X[0]
is
BC
-admissible,

\pi_{n}(LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}(X[0])))=\begin{cases}F(X)&\textrm{if }n=0\\
0&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Remark
A.9
motivates the following definition:

Definition A.10
.

In the situation of
A.4
,
LF
is said to be a
pro-left derived functor
of
F
if for every
X\in\mathbf{B}

\pi_{0}LF(X[0])\cong F(X).

A.5.
BC
-admissible resolution

We will fix the setting of
A.4
. By abuse of notation, we will say that
P\in\mathbf{B}
is
BC
-admissible if
P[0]\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
is.

Proposition A.11
.

Let
P_{\bullet}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
be a strictly bounded complex (see Definition
A.6
) such that
P_{n}
is
BC
-admissible for all
n
. Then
P_{\bullet}
is
BC
-admissible.

Proof.

Up to shift, we can suppose that
P_{\bullet}=\sigma_{\geq 0}P_{\bullet}=\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet}
for some
n\geq 0
: we proceed by induction on
n
.
If
n=0
, then
P_{\bullet}=P_{0}[0]
, and it is
BC
-admissible by assumption.
Let
n>0
and consider the fiber-cofiber sequence in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
:

\sigma_{\leq n-1}P_{\bullet}\to P_{\bullet}\to P_{n}[n].

For all
I\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})
, we conclude by the following diagram in
\mathcal{S}
where the left and right vertical maps are equivalences by induction:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(P_{n}[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}I[-n]))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(P_{\bullet},\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}I))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(\sigma_{\leq n-1}P_{\bullet},\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}I))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(P_{n}[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I[-n]))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(P_{\bullet},i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}(\sigma_{\leq n-1}P_{\bullet},i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

∎

Recall that
\mathbf{B}
is said to be generated by a set of objects
E
if and only if
E
is closed under direct sums and for every
X\in\mathbf{B}
there exists a surjective map

(A.11.1)

P_{0}\to X\to 0

with
P_{0}\in E
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{B}
is generated by a set of objects
E
which are
BC
-admissible. Then let
K
be the kernel of (
A.11.1
), so there exists
P_{1}\in E
together with a surjective map
P_{1}\twoheadrightarrow K
, hence we have an exact sequence:

(A.11.2)

P_{1}\to P_{0}\to X\to 0.

By iterating (
A.11.2
) one can construct a resolution
P_{\bullet}\to X[0]
where
P_{n}\in E
and
P_{n}=0
for
n<0
. We will call this a
connective
BC
-admissible resolution
.

Lemma A.12
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{B}
is generated by a set of objects which are
BC
-admissible. For any
X\in\mathbf{B}
and any connective
BC
-admissible resolution
P_{\bullet}\to X[0]
, we have that

LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}(X[0])\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})).

Proof.

Since
P_{\bullet}\to X[0]
is a resolution, we have
L_{\mathbf{B}}(P_{\bullet})\simeq L_{\mathbf{B}}(X[0])
. Moreover, since
P_{\bullet}
is connective, we have that
P_{\bullet}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet}
, and
\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet}
are
BC
-admissible by Proposition
A.11
. Since
LF
and
L_{\mathbf{B}}
commute with all colimits, by Remark
A.9
we conclude that

LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}X[0])\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})),

∎

We can now prove the main theorem of this appendix:

Theorem A.13
.

In the situation of
A.4
, suppose that
\mathbf{B}
is generated by a set objects which are
BC
-admissible. Then the functor
LF
is a pro-left derived functor of
F
.

Proof.

Let
X\in\mathbf{B}
and
P_{\bullet}\to X[0]
a
BC
-admissible resolution, in particular
P_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{B})_{\geq 0}
. Since
L_{\mathbf{B}}X[0]\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{B})_{\geq 0}
and
LF
is right
t
-exact,
LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}X[0])\in\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})_{\geq 0}
, hence

\pi_{0}LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}X[0])[0]\simeq\tau_{\leq 0}LF(L_{\mathbf{B}}X[0]).

Since
\tau_{\leq 0}
is a left adjoint, it commutes with colimits, so by Lemma
A.12
we have

\tau_{\leq 0}LF(X[0])\simeq\tau_{\leq 0}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\tau_{\leq 0}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})

where the last colimit is computed in
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})_{\leq 0}
. On the other hand, by definition of the
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})
we have

\tau_{\leq 0}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})\simeq L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet}).

For
n\geq 1
, we have that
\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})=\mathrm{coker}(F(P_{1})\to F(P_{0}))[0]
, and since
F
is a left adjoint, it preserves cokernels, so

\mathrm{coker}(F(P_{1})\to F(P_{0}))=F(\mathrm{coker}(P_{1}\to P_{0}))=F(X).

We conclude that in
\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})_{\leq 0}
we have

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\tau_{\leq 0}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(F)(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}F(X)[0]\simeq L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}F(X)[0]

since
L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}F(X)[0]\in\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})^{\heartsuit}
we conclude that

\pi_{0}LF(X[0])\simeq\pi_{0}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\tau_{\leq 0}L_{\mathrm{Pro}\textrm{-}\mathbf{A}}F(\sigma_{\leq n}P_{\bullet}))\simeq F(X).

∎

A.6.

We end this appendix with a criterion of
BC
-admissibility.

Lemma A.14

(See
[
ABV09
, Lemma 2.1.10]
)
.

In the situation of
A.4
, let
P\in\mathbf{B}
such that
P[0]
is compact in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})
. Then
P
is
BC
-admissible if and only if for any injective object
I_{0}\in\mathbf{A}
,
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathbf{B}}(P,G(I_{0}))=0
for
i\neq 0
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
P
is
BC
-admissible. If
I_{0}\in\mathbf{A}
is injective, then
I_{0}[0]
is fibrant, so
I_{0}[0]=i_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}(I_{0}[0])
. Let
I=L_{\mathbf{A}}(I_{0}[0])
, then:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathbf{B}}(P,G(I_{0}))

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],i_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I[-n])).

Since
P[0]
is
BC
-admissible, we have:

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(I[-n]))

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}I[-n])))

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],G(I_{0})[-n])).

The last term is zero for
n\neq 0
, hence
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{B}}(P,G(I_{0}))=0
if
n\neq 0
.

Let us now show the converse implication. We need to show that for every
Y\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{A})
the Beck-Chevalley map induces an equivalence:

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B})}(P[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(Y))\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B})}(P[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}Y)).

Let
I:=i_{\mathbf{A}}(Y)
, so
\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I
is a strictly bounded complex of injectives; since
P[0]
is compact and the colimit in (
A.5.1
) is filtered, Lemma
A.15
below implies that:

(A.14.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(I))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(I)))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I)))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}(\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I)).

Next, observe that, for any
n
and
m
, the map
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I)\to i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I)
is a fibrant replacement (see Remark
A.4
) of bounded complexes, which is given by the total complex of injective resolutions of each
G(I_{r})\to J_{r}^{\bullet}
. So, we have that

(A.14.2)

\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\geq-n}\sigma_{\leq m}I)\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}{\operatorname{Tot}}_{r\in[-n,m]}(J_{r}^{\bullet})={\operatorname{Tot}}_{r\geq-n}(J_{r}^{\bullet})\simeq i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\leq-n}I).

Since
i_{\mathbf{B}}
commutes with filtered colimits by assumption, (
A.14.1
) and (
A.14.2
) imply:

(A.14.3)

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(i_{\mathbf{A}}Y))\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\leq-n}I)).

For every
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have that for
n\gg q
:

\pi_{q}\mathcal{D}(G)(Y)\cong G(\pi_{q}Y)\cong G(\pi_{q}\sigma_{\leq-n}I)\cong\pi_{q}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\leq-n}I),

so since homotopy groups commute with filtered colimits:

(A.14.4)

\pi_{q}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\leq-n}I)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\pi_{q}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma_{\leq-n}I)\cong\pi_{q}\mathcal{D}(G)(Y)

The proof follows then from (
A.14.3
) and (
A.14.4
).
∎

Lemma A.15
.

Let
P\in\mathbf{B}
such that for any injective object
I_{0}\in\mathcal{A}
,
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{B}}(P,G(I_{0}))=0
for
i\neq 0
. Then for any strictly bounded complex
I^{b}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbf{A})

of injective objects of
\mathbf{A}
:

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(G)(I^{b}))\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathbf{B})}(P[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}I^{b})).

Proof.

Let
I^{b}_{n}=0
for
n\notin[-r,s]
. The cofiber of
\sigma_{\leq s-1}I^{b}\to I^{b}
is equivalent to
I^{b}_{s}[s]
: by induction on
r+s
we reduce to
I^{b}=I_{s}[s]
with
I_{s}
an injective object of
\mathcal{B}
. We conclude:

\pi_{n}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{Ch}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(B)}(P[0],i_{\mathbf{B}}\mathcal{D}(G)(L_{\mathbf{A}}I_{s}[s]))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{s-n}_{\mathcal{B}}(P,G(I_{s}))=\begin{cases}0&\textrm{if }n\neq s\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(P,G(I_{s}))&\textrm{if }n=s.\end{cases}

∎
